# Maestro guarantees inventory

Audit for issue [#3105](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/issues/3105) (parent [#3104](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/issues/3104)). **Inventory only**: no Maestro flow was moved, deleted or edited, no production code changed, and no CI trigger, shard count, suite selection or timing-floor policy was touched.

This page answers three questions about the Maestro suite: what actually runs, what each flow costs, and what guarantee each flow owns. Where a flow's guarantee is already owned by a faster tier, the flow is named as a candidate — the migration itself is a follow-up change, not this page's job.

## Method

### The suite was read from the runner, not from the catalogue

The catalogue (`config/docs/maestro-flows.txt`) says which flows *exist*; it does not say what a suite runs. The runner's own selector does, and this audit used that selector:

```bash
python3 scripts/e2e-shard-plan.py --suite smoke --json
python3 scripts/e2e-shard-plan.py --suite full  --json
```

`scripts/e2e-shard-plan.py` imports `emulator.maestro._flow.suite_selection` — the same function `scripts/run-maestro-e2e.py` uses — so the membership below is the runner's, not a re-implementation of it. The shard rule is the runner's too: the tag filter is applied **first**, and shard *N* then holds the flows whose index modulo the shard count is *N-1*.

### The cost came from real runs, not from estimates

Every number in the cost column is a duration Maestro itself wrote into a JUnit report, taken from the artifacts of a real CI run of the `E2E (Maestro)` workflow:

```bash
gh run download 45006143720 --repo gazon1/sing --name maestro-junit-full-1-45006143720-1
```

| Run | Suite | Date | Shards | Flows reported | Flow time (sum of all reports) | Slowest shard (job wall clock) |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| [45006143720](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/45006143720) | full | 2026-11-22 | 4 | 127 | 114.4 min | 46.4 min (shard 1) |
| [44887475720](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/44887475720) | full | 2026-11-21 | 4 | 127 | 88.8 min | 35.7 min (shard 2) |
| [44498782855](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/44498782855) | full | 2026-11-19 | 4 | 127 | 89.9 min | 35.0 min (shard 1) |
| [44060226115](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/44060226115) | full | 2026-11-17 | 4 | 127 | 95.0 min | 39.9 min (shard 2) |
| [45145653332](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/45145653332) | smoke | 2026-11-23 | 4 | 30 | 24.3 min | 13.7 min (shard 1) |

**Total wall-clock duration per shard and per run.** The four shards of a run execute in
parallel, so a run's elapsed time is its **slowest** shard plus queueing — not the sum. The
per-shard job wall clock is the number that bounds the saving of a dropped flow, and it is
what the overhead table below is measured against:

| Run | Suite | Job wall clock per shard (min, shard 1 → 4) | Sum over the four shards | Slowest shard (bounds the run) |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| 45006143720 | full | 46.4 / 44.7 / 42.1 / 35.1 | 168.3 min | 46.4 min |
| 44887475720 | full | 29.3 / 35.7 / 35.7 / 34.6 | 135.3 min | 35.7 min |
| 44498782855 | full | 35.0 / 31.9 / 31.7 / 31.9 | 130.5 min | 35.0 min |
| 44060226115 | full | 35.5 / 39.9 / 32.9 / 32.1 | 140.4 min | 39.9 min |
| 45145653332 | smoke | 13.7 / 12.4 / 10.6 / 9.8 | 46.5 min | 13.7 min |

Each cell is the per-shard job's own wall clock; the sum over the four shards is the
**aggregate** device time a run consumes, not its elapsed time. The per-shard values are
rounded to 0.1 min, so a sum can differ from the total by a tenth of a minute.

**Per-flow cost = the median of the samples in which that flow passed.** A flow that failed in every sampled run has no cost in this document: its recorded time is the time *to the failure*, which is **not** a cost measurement at all — a flow that dies on a timeout can burn more wall clock than a green run, so the recorded time may be shorter or longer than the flow's real cost. Those flows are listed under [the measurement gap](#the-measurement-gap) instead of being given an invented number.

The historical floor in `config/docs/maestro-flow-timings.txt` is **not** used as a runtime: it is a per-flow minimum ever measured, it holds no per-flow entry at all today, and a floor that only ever falls is a lower bound by construction.

### Setup, launch and retry overhead

The job wall clock is larger than the sum of the flow times, and the difference is stable across runs:

| Run | Suite | Per-shard overhead (job wall clock − flow time) |
|---|---|---|
| 45006143720 | full | 14.1 / 14.4 / 14.2 / 11.2 min |
| 44887475720 | full | 11.8 / 11.4 / 12.2 / 11.1 min |
| 44498782855 | full | 10.4 / 9.3 / 10.6 / 10.3 min |
| 44060226115 | full | 11.0 / 13.0 / 11.0 / 10.3 min |
| 45145653332 | smoke | 5.1 / 5.6 / 6.5 / 5.1 min |

That overhead is the AVD restore, the emulator boot, the APK and Maestro installs, and the device reset the runner performs between flows. It is **not** in the flow times below, so the cost column of a row is the flow's own time and nothing else. Dropping a flow therefore removes its seconds **and** its share of the per-flow device reset, but not the shard's fixed overhead (restore, boot, installs) — the shard still has to start.

The runner retries a flow on a **device-level failure** (`maestro._runner`, `--maestro-max-retries`, default 2): either the device died mid-flow, or the failure carried a device-level signature while `probe_device_health()` still answered `alive` — in which case the host was busy rather than the device, and the retry leaves the adb server alone. An assertion failure on a healthy device is **not** retried. A reported duration is therefore one attempt of a flow that was not retried; a retried flow reports only its last attempt.

## The suite, as the runner sees it

| Suite | Flows | Shards | Shard sizes | What selects it |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| `smoke` | 30 | 4 | 8 / 8 / 7 / 7 | every flow that declares the `smoke` tag, wherever it lives |
| `full` | 127 | 4 | 32 / 32 / 32 / 31 | every catalogue flow except those tagged `manual` or `auth-live` |
| excluded | 8 | — | — | tagged `manual` or `auth-live` — run by hand, never by CI |

The catalogue holds 135 flows. The shard count comes from `config/docs/e2e-shards.txt` (`full 4`, `smoke 4`).

**The shard file's own comment is stale.** `config/docs/e2e-shards.txt` still explains the
smoke suite as "the same 27 flows between them in shares of 7, 7, 7 and 6". The runner's
selection today gives **30** flows in shares of **8 / 8 / 7 / 7** — the comment predates the
flows that carry the `smoke` tag. The file's *configuration* is right (both suites are still
split four ways), and its own rule — "only the count belongs here" — is what this audit
followed; the stale part is the prose around it. Correcting that comment is a separate
documentation-only change and is **not** done here: this audit changes no configuration file.

**Reading the Shard column.** Each inventory row carries the shard the flow actually runs in:
`S1`–`S4` is a shard of the `smoke` suite, `F1`–`F4` a shard of the `full` suite, `S2/F4` a flow that
runs in **both** suites and lands in a different shard in each, and `—` an excluded flow that no shard
ever runs. The assignment is the runner's own rule — the suite's flows are sorted, and flow *i* goes
to shard `(i % shard count) + 1` — so it is derived from the same
`select_suite_flows` the runner uses and was cross-checked against
`python3 scripts/e2e-shard-plan.py --suite {smoke,full} --shard N --json` for all eight shards.
Dropping a flow therefore removes its seconds from one shard, or from one shard of **each** suite if
it runs in both.

**The excluded eight** — the `capture` flows are tagged `manual` (they need a human to place the capture intent) and the two network-failure auth flows are tagged `auth-live` (the CI APK is built with a stub auth provider and has no live provider to fail against):

| Flow | Tags |
|---|---|
| `Maestro/flows/capture/01-today.yaml` | `manual`, `capture` |
| `Maestro/flows/capture/02-task-detail.yaml` | `manual`, `capture` |
| `Maestro/flows/capture/03-project-detail.yaml` | `manual`, `capture` |
| `Maestro/flows/capture/04-upcoming.yaml` | `manual`, `capture` |
| `Maestro/flows/capture/05-calendar.yaml` | `manual`, `capture` |
| `Maestro/flows/capture/06-notes.yaml` | `manual`, `capture` |
| `Maestro/flows/auth/08-signup-network-error.yaml` | `auth-live`, `scenario:AUTH-FAIL-01`, `manual` |
| `Maestro/flows/auth/09-signin-network-error.yaml` | `auth-live`, `scenario:AUTH-FAIL-02`, `manual` |

They have no measurement and no automated owner, and that is by design.

## The inventory

One row per flow. The **cost** column is the median of the passing samples above; the **disposition** column is one of:

| Disposition | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `keep` | The flow owns a guarantee no faster tier owns — usually a platform-only one. |
| `split` | Part of its assertions are behaviour-only and could move to a headless tier; the rest must stay on-device. |
| `replace-candidate` | Every guarantee the flow provides is already owned by an existing headless test; a follow-up could drop the flow. |
| `investigate` | Overlap or ownership is plausible but not proven; the gap is named. |

The per-flow **existing coverage** column names only test classes that were read in the tree during this audit — never a name inferred from a scenario id.

**All 135 catalogue flows are classified below.** The eight excluded flows are in
[the excluded eight](#the-suite-as-the-runner-sees-it); the 127 automated ones are in the
area tables:

| Disposition | Rows | of which automated flows | Measured flow time they carry |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `keep` | 24 | 22 | 769 s (12.8 min) |
| `split` | 70 | 70 | 2930 s (48.8 min) |
| `replace-candidate` | 27 | 27 | 966 s (16.1 min) |
| `investigate` | 8 | 8 | 55 s (0.9 min) |
| **total** | **129** | **127** | **4720 s (78.7 min)** |

The times are the medians of the passing samples, so they are what the flows cost on a
green run; the 23 flows that never passed carry no number at all and are counted in
[the measurement gap](#the-measurement-gap). The column carries the seconds because they
add up exactly and the rounded minutes do not: the four rows sum to 78.6 min, the total is
78.7 min.

There are **129 rows, not 129 flows**: the 127 automated flows have a row each, and the eight
excluded flows share two rows (the six `capture` flows, and the two `auth-live` flows). That is
why the row count and the catalogue count differ by six.

The `keep` row therefore counts **22 automated flows plus the two aggregated excluded rows**.
Those two rows are `keep` in the sense of "no shard runs them and no automated tier owns
them" — they are not a classification of an automated flow, and they carry no time. Every
other disposition covers automated flows only.

### Task sub-features: checklist, logbook, subtasks

*`Maestro/flows/tasks/checklist/`, `…/logbook/`, `…/subtasks/`*

| Flow | Shard | Suite/tags | Cost (s, measured) | Journey & assertions | Platform guarantees | Existing coverage | Disposition | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tasks/checklist/TASK-CHECK-01.yaml` | S1/F3 | smoke+full; `scenario:TASK-CHECK-01`,`smoke`,`tasks` | 58 s (n=3) | launch-clean → `nav_tab_today` → wait `tasks_fab` → tap FAB → type `${RUN_ID} Grocery run` into `task_editor_title_input` → `task_editor_save` → wait/tap `task_item_${RUN_ID}_grocery_run` → `scrollUntilVisible task_checklist_section` → tap the header → wait `checklist_inline_add_input` → type `${RUN_ID} Milk` → `assertVisible checklist_inline_add_input` (IME-open field stays on screen, UXF-03/#2874) → tap `checklist_inline_add_button` → wait `checklist_item_${RUN_ID}_milk` → tap `checklist_item_checkbox_${RUN_ID}_milk` → wait `checked: true` | `tasks_fab` is an **Android-shell** tag (`shared/src/androidMain/.../AndroidShellNav3.kt`; the desktop shell's FAB has no such id); the real IME keeping the field on screen; the resource-id layer (`testTagsAsResourceId = true` at the root of `shared/src/androidMain/.../App.kt` — the desktop actual of `mapTestTagsAsResourceIds()` is a no-op, so `id:` selectors are device-only) | `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/tasks/TaskItemListScenarioTest.kt` (`TASK-ITEM-01 …`) — opens the checklist section, ticks `checklistItemCheckbox`, asserts "Checklist (1/2)", adds an item inline through `CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_INPUT`+`CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_BUTTON`, asserts `CHECKLIST_SHEET` does not open; `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/tasks/TaskDetailEmptyChildSectionsTest.kt` — checklist header present while empty, inline add-row present, count "Checklist (0/1)", empty hint gone after the add; `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/ChecklistAndSubtasksSectionUiTest.kt` — "the checklist label reports the completed count", "a checklist item's title is inert and its checkbox is the toggle"; `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/checklist/ChecklistOrderingTest.kt`, `ChecklistRestoreTest.kt`, `ChecklistReadIsolationTest.kt`; `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/TaskDetailImeInsetsTest.kt` (device) — `focused_task_detail_fields_stay_above_ime` already covers the IME guarantee for `CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_INPUT` | split | every *behaviour* the flow asserts (section present and openable, inline add-row, item appears, checkbox toggles, count) is already owned by the desktop tier (`TaskItemListScenarioTest` asserts strictly more, including the count the flow deliberately does not assert). The device-only halves are the FAB→editor path on the Android shell, the real IME, and the resource-id layer — and the IME half is *already* owned by `TaskDetailImeInsetsTest`. The flow is currently failing in 2 of 5 sampled runs (2026-11-21 and 2026-11-23) and has no passing sample on the smoke lane |

the real IME in the note title field; the resource-id layer | `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/notes/NotesRepositoryLogbookAttachmentTest.kt` (real in-memory SQLite) — `createForTask writes the attachment onto the note`, `the attached note is in the task's logbook right after it is created`, `a content save does not drop the attachment — the editor's autosave path`, `the note is still in the task's logbook after a content save`, `a note of another task is not in this task's logbook`; `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/tasks/TaskRepositoryContractTest.kt` — `observeSubtasks returns direct children ordered by sortOrder then createdAt`, `reorderSubtasks renumbers …`; `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/tasks/domain/usecase/CreateTaskAttributeCarryOverTest.kt` — the door-by-door carry-over guard whose header names the #171 defect class ("`parentTaskId` was omitted from one door and silently turned 'add subtask' into 'add a top-level task'") | split | the flow's own spec names #171 as the motivating defect ("CreateTaskUseCase … silently dropped it at the use-case boundary") and that fix **is** covered headlessly — `TaskChildrenSlotTest.add subtask parents it to the current task` walks `TaskChildrenSlot.addSubtask` → `CreateTaskInput(parentTaskId = parent.id)` → the fake repository and asserts the relation landed. Add, toggle, delete and undo are all owned at the slot level. The delete half has no *UI* owner though: `TestTags.subtaskDelete(title)` is asserted by no test (only `TaskItemListScenarioTest`/`ChecklistAndSubtasksSectionUiTest` touch the checkbox). ### Tasks disposition histogram

| Disposition | Count | Flows |
|---|---|---|
| keep | 7 | 05-archive-via-menu, 06-delete-undo, 07-cyrillic-title, 08-date-buckets, 16-return-to-source-tab, 17-create-daily-recurring, create-task |
| split | 7 | 01-set-priority, 02-set-due-date, 04-delete, 13-create-full-task, 18-time-tracking-start-stop, 20-planning-deadline, 22-planning-someday |
| replace-candidate | 6 | 03-due-date-cancel, 09-rename-empty, 14-prefill-from-today, 15-prefill-from-inbox, 19-prefill-from-upcoming, toggle-task-complete |
| investigate | 3 | 10-swipe-delete-undo, 21-planning-reminder, open-task-detail |

Measured cost of the 17 flows that are green in the window: **686 s of median device time**
(14–50 s each). The 6 flows with no passing sample cost nothing today, but they also
guarantee nothing — 6 of 23 flows in this area are currently dead weight in the suite
(`09`, `13`, `14`, `21`, `create-task`, `open-task-detail`); all six run in the `full` suite,
and two of them (`09`, `create-task`) also run in `smoke`. (`10` is not among them: it has a
single passing sample, 32 s, from the 2026-11-17 run.)

That is the local-first promise in one sentence, so the flow stays whole. |

The *restore* half — `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.Unarchive` → `TaskLifecycleSlot.unarchive()` → `TaskRepository.restore` → the task is back in Inbox — has **no owner I found**; the device test's own header explicitly leaves the restore to issue #2723. That half is the cheapest new headless owner in this area. Note the flow carries no `scenario:` tag although `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/archive/ARCHIVE-01.yaml` names it as the carrier. |
| `archive/02-bulk-archive-confirm.yaml` | S4/F2 | smoke+full; `smoke`,`archive` (no `scenario:` tag) | 81 s (n=5) | seed → long-press the row → mark as completed → the Archive screen shows `archive_empty` → the old full-width button is **not** visible → overflow → bulk action → `dialog_confirm` → Cancel → the task is still a live task in Inbox → Archive again → overflow → bulk action → confirm → "Moved 1 task to archive" result dialog → dismiss it → the archived row is listed | the long-press context-menu sheet (`task_context_menu_sheet`); the archive overflow as a popup; the confirm dialog's resource id; the **result dialog is a modal whose window covers the list** (the flow says so) | `ArchiveBulkActionFlowTest` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/archive/`) — `the_bulk_archive_confirms_before_writing` (overflow → confirm → **Cancel writes nothing**, read through the repository → confirm → the row appears), `the_confirmation_does_not_reappear_after_eligibility_returns`, `the_action_is_disabled_without_eligible_tasks`; `ArchiveViewModelTest` — `the eligible count is the completed live tasks`, `a successful archive reports the repository's count`, `a single archived task is reported in the singular` ("Moved 1 task to archive"). **No class carries a bulk-archive scenario id** | **split** | The behaviour half of the flow is already owned by a desktop twin that is *strictly stronger*: it reads the repository write instead of trusting the observation, checks the count in the confirm button's label ("Archive 1 task"), and asserts the disabled state without eligible tasks. The residue is genuinely device-only and **has no owner today**: the long-press context sheet (a platform interaction no JVM tier has), the overflow popup's resource ids and the result dialog's modal window. A follow-up may move the behaviour half into a headless tier, but the flow itself stays until a device owner exists for the residue — so this is a `split`, not a `replace-candidate`. |

### Menu, navigation, system

*`Maestro/flows/menu/`, `…/nav/`, `…/system/`*

| Flow | Shard | Suite/tags | Cost (s, measured) | Journey & assertions | Platform guarantees | Existing coverage | Disposition | Rationale |

| `menu/01-projects-roots.yaml` | F2 | full only · menu, scenario:MENU-08 | 88 s (n=3) | Seed Parent → Child (`parent=parent`) → Grandchild (`parent=child`) → open menu → unfold the section → `menu_project_node_parent` visible → swipe the sheet to its end → `menu_project_node_child` and `menu_project_node_grandchild` **not** visible → tap the root → `shell_bar_title` = "Parent" → `project_detail_children_chip` → `project_child_row_child` → tap → title "Child" → its own children chip → `project_child_row_grandchild` → title "Grandchild" → back → "Child" → back → "Parent" → back → "Projects" | **Android-unique by construction**: the sheet lists roots only (ADR `docs/decisions/2026-11-12-android-menu-roots-only.md`); the children live behind the parent's own screen, in `ChildProjectsSheet` — a `ModalBottomSheet`, i.e. a **window-owning surface** whose rows need `mapTestTagsAsResourceIds()` to become resource ids (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/projects/presentation/components/ChildProjectsSheet.kt:43,67`); the back-out symmetry is the Android Nav3 shell's `SHELL_BACK_BUTTON` (`AndroidShellNav3.kt:308,331,341`) | `D/feature/projects/presentation/components/ProjectTreeSectionModeTest.kt` (three tests: "the list of roots does not draw the children", "the list of roots offers no expander", "the tree draws the children and their expander") — covers the roots-only *drawing switch* only; `S/feature/projects/presentation/screen/ProjectDetailTopBarShellPublishTest.kt` (the shell title publisher); no class covers the drill-down chain or the back-out symmetry | **keep** | The only flow that proves the whole chain the ADR created: sheet → root → parent's screen → its children sheet → child's screen → back out. The desktop drawer keeps the full tree inline (`DesktopShellNav3.kt`, `ProjectsMenuSection(..., onToggleExpanded = { … })`), so no headless tier has an equivalent journey to copy. It is also the slowest flow in the area (88 s) and failed on 2026-11-22 — worth a flake review, but dropping it would drop the ADR's own guarantee. |
| `menu/02-calendar-entry.yaml` | F3 | full only · menu, scenario:MENU-04 | 23 s (n=4) | Open the app → tap `nav_tab_calendar` → `calendar_month_title` visible → open the menu → `menu_calendar` **not** visible | The bar's Calendar tab is an Android-shell resource id (`nav_tab_calendar`, applied only in `AndroidShellNav3.kt:150`); `menu_calendar` is a *declared-but-never-applied* tag (`core/ui/TestTags.kt:192`, listed as known-unapplied in `SJ/arch/TestTagsWiringTest.kt`) | `D/feature/flows/menu/ProjectsMenuCalendarScenarioTest.kt` (the desktop carrier of MENU-04) — but it asserts that the desktop **drawer** shows a "Calendar" entry, which it does because `DesktopShellNav3` draws `DestinationKind.tabs` (which includes `Calendar`) before `AppMenu.destinations`; `S/shell/AppMenuTest.kt` (`theCalendarIsATabAndThePomodoroIsInTheMenu`) | **investigate** | The flow and its own desktop carrier assert **opposite things** about the same scenario id. The flow says "the Calendar is a tab and the sheet no longer lists it"; the desktop carrier says "the drawer lists a Calendar entry". Both can pass, because the drawer draws tabs and the sheet draws `AppMenu.sheetDestinations` — but then the desktop carrier does not test what the flow tests, and the traceability spec `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/menu/MENU-04.yaml` ("Calendar is listed in the menu on both platforms") is stale relative to the model. The flow's own negative assertion (`assertNotVisible: menu_calendar`) is close to vacuous: the id is never applied anywhere, so it cannot fail for a behavioural reason. The owner must decide which surface owns MENU-04 before this flow is kept or replaced. |

The rename half is owned by **nobody** — a follow-up would have to add it to the desktop class before the flow could go. The device residue is the sheet's reopen-and-read path — a surface property of the modal sheet, not a behaviour. The flow is the second slowest in the area (64 s). |

It therefore guarantees nothing today. Two candidate causes, not distinguishable without running the app: (a) the `session=anonymous` seed link does not land the session before the sheet is composed, or (b) the sheet was composed while the session was still `SignedIn` and the gate never re-evaluated. The gate itself is **unowned by any Kotlin test** — `AppMenuTest` checks the entry exists, not when it appears — which is the more valuable finding than the flake. |

The flow's screenshot is its only unique residue, and the sheet's window/bounds are already covered device-side. One of the cheapest flows in the area (18 s — `nav/01-no-calendar-tab` is 13 s) and green in all five sampled runs. |

### Smoke, settings, sync, profile, pomodoro

*`Maestro/flows/smoke/`, `…/settings/`, `…/sync/`, `…/profile/`, `…/pomodoro/`*

| Flow | Shard | Suite/tags | Cost (s, measured) | Journey & assertions | Platform guarantees | Existing coverage | Disposition | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `smoke/01-launch-today.yaml` | S2/F4 | smoke+full; `smoke` (no `scenario:` tag) | 13 s (n=5) | cold start → `tasks_fab`, `nav_tab_inbox`, `nav_tab_today`, `nav_tab_upcoming`, `nav_tab_calendar`, `nav_menu_button` are all visible | the **Android** bottom bar of `AndroidShellNav3` and the test-tag→resource-id mapping; the desktop shell is a different shell with a different destination set | `NavigationSmokeTest` (`androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/`) — `app_launches_and_all_tabs_are_reachable`, `fab_visibility_matches_tab_contract`; `AppStartupTest.the_shell_is_addressable_by_test_tag` (device tier) | keep | the ownership table's *shell / navigation reachability* row keeps "the Maestro smoke flow … as the device-level entry check" on purpose; the flow is the cheapest in the area (13 s) and is the one place where the whole Android shell is observed after a real cold start |
| `smoke/02-menu-settings.yaml` | S3/F3 | smoke+full; `smoke`,`settings` (no `scenario:` tag) | 23 s (n=4) | menu sheet → scroll `menu_settings` into view → tap → `settings_tab_dropdown` visible → system **back** → `nav_tab_today` visible again | the Android menu **bottom sheet** (row below the fold, scrolled by the flow); the narrow-settings trigger `settings_tab_dropdown` (<600 dp only); the system back gesture returning to the agenda | `MenuParityFlowTest` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/menu/`) — NAV-02 model parity, every menu entry offered; `MenuSheetBoundsTest` (`androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/`) — sheet bounds and reachability; `SyncSettings01ScenarioTest.change_interval_cycles_value` (opens Settings from the drawer) | split | "Settings opens from the menu" is headless-owned (the desktop twin opens the same destination from the drawer); the device-only residue is the sheet row's reachability and the system-back return, neither of which any JVM tier can observe |

*`Maestro/flows/calendar/`, `…/calendar_sync/`, `…/lifecycle/`, `…/capture/`*

What no faster tier has is the Android tab and the pager's duplicate-cell behaviour |

The residue is the flow's `id:`-addressed surface — `calendar_sync_system_enable_switch`, `calendar_sync_provider_google_calendar`, `calendar_sync_google_connect_button` are resource ids no headless tier renders — and the Android **grant** path behind the connect button. That residue has **no owner today**: the desktop tier deliberately asserts the *absence* of the system switch (`CalendarSyncSystemScenarioTest` → `assertTagNotExists(SYSTEM_ENABLE_SWITCH)`), because desktop has no system calendar. So the flow cannot be dropped whole — it is a `split`, not a `replace-candidate`. It also fails in every sampled run with `calendar_sync_google_connect_button is visible`, i.e. the failure is in the Google-grant half, not in the swap |

The registered cause is `Assertion is false: id: calendar_sync_system_unavailable is visible` (issue #835, `owner: unclassified`, `status: accepted`). The guarantee it names is owned by the desktop twin; the flow is a *desktop* scenario wearing an Android costume |

The VM tier owns the enable/disable *state transition*; only a device owns the switch |

The device-only half is the *process* being killed and the app coming back — no headless tier kills a process |
| `lifecycle/03-rotate-in-editor.yaml` | F1 | full; `regression`,`lifecycle` | — (fails in every sampled run) | seed a task → open the editor → rotate to landscape → the editor is still there and the title survived → rotate back → the editor is still there | a **configuration change** and the recomposition after it; `setOrientation` is a device action with no desktop or JVM equivalent (a grep for `setOrientation`/`ConfigurationChange` over `shared/src`, `androidApp/src`, `desktopApp/src` found only production code and the flow itself) | **none found** — `TaskEditorDraftTest` (`androidApp/src/androidTest/`) covers draft restoration across a *close*, not across a rotation | keep | it is the only carrier of the configuration-change guarantee anywhere in the repository, and it is the only reason to keep a lifecycle flow for the editor. It fails in every sampled run with `task_editor_title_input is visible` (issue #837, `status: accepted`) — a registered cause, and the flow's own text says the editor is asserted as text because no task row is mounted |

They are excluded from CI by tag, have no cost and no owner, and this audit leaves them exactly as they are |
| `auth/08-signup-network-error.yaml`, `auth/09-signin-network-error.yaml` | — | excluded — `auth-live`,`manual` | not measured | sign-up / sign-in against a provider that fails on the network | a **live** auth provider, which the CI APK does not carry (it is built with a stub provider) | none — by design | keep | same as the capture flows: excluded on purpose, unmeasured, untouched by this audit |

## The measurement gap

Three things in the measured suite are not a measurement, and this page does not pretend
they are.

### 23 flows have no cost at all

These flows failed in **every** sampled full run, so no passing sample exists and the
cost column reads "—":

`agenda/01-smart-lists`, `attachments/01-add-url`, `attachments/02-delete`,
`attachments/04-persistence`, `auth/05-session-invalidation`, `backup/02-cloud-password`,
`calendar_sync/01-provider-toggle`, `calendar_sync/02-system-calendar-unavailable`,
`lifecycle/03-rotate-in-editor`, `menu/09-attach-account-entry`, `negative/05-empty-search`,
`projects/04-pick-emoji`, `projects/05-custom-emoji`, `search/02-cyrillic`,
`search/07-saved-search`, `search/12-no-results-recovery`, `search/13-suggestion-dismissal`,
`tasks/09-rename-empty`, `tasks/13-create-full-task`, `tasks/14-prefill-from-today`,
`tasks/21-planning-reminder`, `tasks/create-task`, `tasks/open-task-detail`.

What the JUnit report holds for such a flow is the time **to the failure**, not the flow's
cost. The runner retries only on a device-level failure, so a reported duration is one
non-retried attempt and the attempt stopped early. Giving these flows a number would be inventing it.

### The floor passes and the gate still fails

The floor (`config/docs/maestro-failure-floor.txt`: `full 30`, `smoke 4`) counts failing
flows. Running the repository's own gate over the downloaded artifacts
(`scripts/ci/check-maestro-results.py`) says something the floor cannot:

| Run | Scope | Failing flows | Floor | Gate verdict |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| [45006143720](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/45006143720) (11-22) | full | 27 | 30 | **failed** — 16 causes not in the registry, 2 causes marked `fixed` came back |
| [44887475720](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/44887475720) (11-21) | full | 49 | 30 | **failed** — 13 causes not in the registry, 1 `fixed` cause came back; the 49 are over the floor |
| [44498782855](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/44498782855) (11-19) | full | 27 | 30 | **failed** — 16 causes not in the registry, 2 `fixed` causes came back |
| [44060226115](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/44060226115) (11-17) | full | 28 | 30 | **failed** — 18 causes not in the registry, 1 `fixed` cause came back |
| [45145653332](https://github.com/gazon1/sing/actions/runs/45145653332) (11-23) | smoke | 12 | 4 | **failed** — over the floor, 2 causes not in the registry, 2 `fixed` causes came back |

Every sampled run failed its own gate. In three of the four full runs the failure count was
**under** the floor, so the red comes from the second half of the contract: `config/docs/maestro-debt.yaml`
is the *cause* registry, and a run that fails on a cause the registry does not list fails
the gate even when the count is within the floor. Thirteen to seventeen causes per run are
not in it.

Three causes come back that the registry marks `fixed`: `#919`
(`"https://example.com/render-test" is visible`), `#842` (`snackbar_action`) and `#927`
(`nav_tab_today is visible`).

### One shared helper precondition is the largest single cause

`Assertion is false: id: nav_tab_today is visible` is asserted by three **shared helpers** —
`Maestro/helpers/launch-clean.yaml`, `Maestro/helpers/seed.yaml` and
`Maestro/helpers/session-signedin.yaml` — so it is a precondition every flow inherits. When
the app's shell does not come up, the whole shard dies with one message: 32 flows in shard 1
of the 11-21 run, and all 7 flows of shard 3 in the 11-23 smoke run. The two runs that are
"over the floor" are over it for this reason alone, and a per-flow reading of the cost table
cannot see it — it is a per-*shard* event.

One note on the registry itself: its `environment` block still says "the Maestro shards are
skipped in CI — there is no emulator there", which was true when it was written. The
`E2E (Maestro)` workflow now runs the shards on an emulator (AVD `singularity-api36`), and
the registry carries a matching `environments:` entry, so the gate does compare the CI runs
against a calibration — it is the prose above it that has fallen behind.

## The ranked candidate set

The ranking answers one question: **which flows could a follow-up drop, because a headless test
already owns their guarantee today?** It is an **editorial** order, not a strict sort: it weighs

1. how completely the guarantee is owned headlessly, and how clean that ownership claim is — a
   `replace-candidate` row means the **whole flow** could be dropped; a `split` row means only its
   **behaviour half** could, because the device residue has no owner today (the residue is named
   in the row);
2. how much measured device time the flow carries;
3. confidence in the ownership claim — every owner below was **read in the tree** during this
   audit, and the class and the test method are named;
4. **implementation effort** — what a follow-up has to do before the flow can go.

**How this maps onto the issue's three ranking criteria.** The issue asks for a ranking by
*expected CI time saved*, *confidence in equivalence* and *implementation effort*. Criterion 2
above is the time, criterion 3 is the confidence and criterion 4 is the effort; criterion 1 is
the **precondition** the other three are weighed inside: a flow whose guarantee nobody owns
cannot be dropped, so its cost and effort are not savings and work — they are a bill for a new
test. That is why ownership completeness comes first, and it is also why the order is editorial
rather than a numeric sort — the three criteria are not commensurable (a `split` row's saving is
unknown, as shown below).

**Effort** is not a guess about calendar time; it is read off what the follow-up needs:

| Effort | What the follow-up must do first |
|---|---|
| **low** | the headless owner exists and is strictly stronger — delete the flow, update the catalogue and the scenario link |
| **medium** | a headless owner exists but a **gap in it** must be closed first (a missing half of an existing test class, or a fake that must be extended) |
| **high** | the guarantee has **no owner at all** — a new headless test (or a new device owner for the residue) must be written before the flow can shrink |

**Cost is flow time, not shard time.** The numbers are the medians of the passing samples; the
per-shard overhead (AVD restore, emulator boot, install, per-flow device reset — 9–14 min per full
shard, 5–7 min per smoke shard) is not in them, so a dropped flow saves less wall clock than its
row suggests, and a dropped flow in a `smoke` flow saves it in **both** suites.

**Cost is the flow's whole time, not the part that a follow-up would save.** For a
`replace-candidate` row the whole flow time is removable. For a `split` row only the behaviour
half's share of it is, and that share is **not measured** — the per-flow times cannot tell how
much of a flow's seconds belong to its behaviour half and how much to its device residue — so the
saving of a `split` row is **unknown**, and its cost column is an upper bound, never a forecast.

### The largest `split` payoffs

These flows must **stay** — each owns a platform-only half. The payoff is in moving the
behaviour-only half out, and the saving is the part of the flow that the headless tier can take
over, not the whole flow. **How much of a flow's time that part is, is unknown** — the per-flow
measurements cannot split a flow's seconds between its behaviour half and its device residue — so
the cost column is the flow's **whole** time and the saving of each row is **unknown**, never
smaller than zero and never the cost column.

| Flow | Cost (whole flow) | Effort | What the headless tier could take | What must stay on-device |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| `agenda/06-full-lifecycle.yaml` | 99 s | high — no owner exists for the composition itself | the **composition** (empty → create → card → results → edit → rename → delete → empty); each step is already owned, the composition is not | the overflow popup's resource ids and the real IME |
| `tasks/logbook/TASK-LOG-01.yaml` | 80 s | high — the two tags are asserted by no test in the tree | the "+" → the attached note appears in the task's logbook (the `task_id`-loss bug is owned three times over, once on real SQLite); the two tags `LOGBOOK_ADD_NOTE_BUTTON` and `logbookNoteByTitle(...)` are asserted by **no** test in the tree | the cross-graph exit (`TasksNavigator.openCreateNote` leaves the tasks graph) and the IME |
| `auth/04-signin-with-seeded-data.yaml` | 77 s | medium | the seeded-data half of sign-in | the live sign-in path itself |
| `auth/10-attach-account-validation-error.yaml` | 75 s | medium | the validation rule | the attach-account entry surface |
| `auth/07-account-attach.yaml` | 70 s | medium | the attach flow's outcome | the account surface |

### How to act on this list

Every row above is a **proposal for a follow-up change issue**, not a change made by this audit.
A follow-up that acts on a `replace-candidate` must do three things in one commit: name the
owning test, drop the flow, and update the flow catalogue and the scenario link — otherwise the
scenario id loses its carrier and the traceability check reports a gap.

The `split` rows are different in kind: they ask for a **new** headless test for a guarantee
that has no owner today (the logbook's "+" button, the search empty-state tags, the tags empty
state). Those are additions to the headless tier, not deletions from the device tier, and they
buy nothing on the nightly's wall clock until the flow's behaviour-only half is dropped from it.

The **Effort** column says which kind of change a row needs. Start with the `low` rows: the owner
exists and is strictly stronger, so the follow-up is a deletion plus a catalogue and scenario-link
update. The `medium` rows need a gap closed in an existing owner first; the `high` rows need a
guarantee written from scratch, and they buy **no** wall clock at all until that work lands.

## What this audit does not claim

- It does not move or delete a flow. Every candidate below is a proposal for a follow-up change issue.
- It does not claim that a headless test is equivalent to a flow: a `replace-candidate` means the **guarantee** has an owner today, and the owner is named.
- It does not change the failure floor, the shard count, the suite selection or the timing-floor policy. The nightly's cost stays what it is until a follow-up acts on the candidate list.
- It does not treat the 23 flows that fail in every sampled run as a measurement: their duration is a time-to-failure, and their cost is unknown until they pass.

## Appendix A — the candidate detail, per flow

Every flow marked `split` or `replace-candidate` above, with the behaviour-only assertions, the production boundary it exercises, the tier that already owns it, the assertions that must stay on-device, and a confidence note.

### Agenda and empty states

### `agenda/01-smart-lists.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the undated task is bucketed under "No Date" in the Inbox preset; dating it moves it out of Inbox and into Today; it is not on Today before it is dated.
- **Production boundary**: `AgendaEvaluator.evaluate` + `AgendaPresets.Inbox` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/agenda/domain/logic/`) and the agenda coordinator `AgendaViewModel` (`.../presentation/viewmodel/AgendaViewModel.kt`), driven by `AgendaIntent` (`.../domain/model/AgendaIntent.kt`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/agenda/domain/AgendaTabDisjointnessTest.kt` and `AgendaInboxTabMeaningTest.kt` already own the domain half; the missing piece is the *coordinator* half (a task whose due date changes ends up on another tab) — a `shared/src/jvmTest` test on `AgendaViewModel` with the fake repository (`shared/src/test/fakes/`) would close it in seconds.
- **Must stay on-device**: the scheduling sheet's `task_scheduling_*` tags inside a modal bottom sheet, the Android bottom-bar `nav_tab_*` switch, the debug-seed deep link, and the system-back exit from the editor.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the flow's own text says the three tabs are disjoint; a regression that widens the Inbox preset (or a coordinator that keeps a dated task in the Inbox bucket) breaks the product's core tab model and no device-specific property is involved. The desktop twin for the sheet already exists (`TaskSchedulingSheetTest`).
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the split; the flow is also *failing* in every sampled run and contradicts `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/agenda/AGENDA-01.yaml`, so the spec text must be reconciled with the code before anything is moved.

### `agenda/02-saved-views-open.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: the saved-views list renders its empty state ("No saved views yet") and offers the create FAB; back returns to the agenda.
- **Production boundary**: `SavedAgendaListScreen` / `SavedAgendaContent` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/agenda/presentation/screen/`), reached through `AgendaNavigator.openSavedAgendaList()` (`.../presentation/nav/AgendaNavigator.kt:100`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/agenda/SavedViewsFlowTest.kt` — `the_saved_views_list_opens_and_reports_its_empty_state` and `leaving_the_saved_views_list_returns_to_the_agenda` already assert exactly these; `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../test/helpers/SavedViewsRobot.kt` is the fixture.
- **Must stay on-device**: whether the Android shell's own bar button overlaps the list's create FAB (the desktop test header records this was an Android-only defect — `the_saved_views_list_carries_no_shell_button` guards the desktop shell only).
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a blank list instead of the empty state is a shared-UI defect, caught in seconds instead of a 21 s device run.
- **Confidence**: high.

### `agenda/03-saved-views-crud.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: Save-current pre-fills the name with the agenda title; clearing and typing a name persists it; Save pops the editor back to the agenda (`SavedAgendaEvent.SaveSuccess` → `Notification.NavigateBack`, `SavedAgendaScreen.kt:124`); the card appears in the list; edit-mode Delete raises the "Delete view?" confirm and removes the card.
- **Production boundary**: `SavedAgendaScreen` (editor) + `SavedAgendaViewModel` (`.../presentation/viewmodel/SavedAgendaViewModel.kt`), `SavedAgendaListScreen` + `SavedAgendaListViewModel` (`.../presentation/viewmodel/SavedAgendaListViewModel.kt`), persistence in `SavedAgendaViewsRepositoryImpl` (Room `AgendaViewDao`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/agenda/SavedAgendaCreateFlowTest.kt` (save pops the editor and the view is persisted) and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/agenda/SavedViewsFlowTest.kt` (`delete_from_the_editor_removes_the_card_from_the_list`, `overflow_menu_shows_edit_and_delete`) and `SavedAgendaEditFlowTest.kt`.
- **Must stay on-device**: the DropdownMenu is a popup window of its own, so its rows reach UiAutomator only through `mapTestTagsAsResourceIds` (`SavedAgendaCard.kt:107`, `AgendaContent.kt:471`) — no faster tier can observe that; and the real IME text entry (`eraseText` / `inputText`).
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: "Save spins forever" (the VM not clearing `isSaving`) and "delete does not confirm" were both real regressions; both are pure coordinator contracts and are already asserted on desktop.
- **Confidence**: high.

### `agenda/04-saved-view-results.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only**: the card opens a *results* screen whose contents match the saved definition (a stored filter, not a stored label); the title is the view's name; the matching task is listed.
- **Production boundary**: `AgendaNavigator.openSavedAgendaResults(viewId)` (`.../presentation/nav/AgendaNavigator.kt:105`) and `SavedAgendaListScreen.onViewSelected`; evaluation by `AgendaEvaluator` over the view's `sectionsJson`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/agenda/OpenSavedViewShowsMatchingTasksFlowTest.kt` — its header states it is the desktop mirror of this very flow; plus `SavedViewsFlowTest.card_tap_navigates_to_results_and_back_shows_list` and `SavedAgendaEditFlowTest.card_tap_opens_results_not_editor`. The spec `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/agenda/AGENDA-04.yaml` names this distinction as the point of the scenario, and the desktop class already carries it.
- **Must stay on-device**: the Android overflow-menu resource ids and the real IME name entry — but both are already exercised by `03-saved-views-crud.yaml`, so nothing is lost if this flow is dropped.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: "a view that renders its own title over an unfiltered list" passes a shallow flow; only an assertion on the *contents* separates the two, and the desktop class makes exactly that assertion.
- **Confidence**: high.

### `agenda/06-full-lifecycle.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: empty → create → results → rename → reopen → delete → empty, with no intermediate state stranding the user; the rename is what the list shows afterwards; both list-level and edit-mode delete raise the confirm.
- **Production boundary**: the saved-view chain `SavedAgendaListScreen` → `SavedAgendaScreen`/`SavedAgendaViewModel` → `SavedAgendaViewsRepository` (Room), and `AgendaNavigator.openSavedAgendaResults` / `openSavedAgendaEdit`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/agenda/SavedViewsFlowTest.kt`, `SavedAgendaEditFlowTest.kt` and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/agenda/SavedAgendaCreateFlowTest.kt` — the pieces; the composition guard would be one new compose test over the same three.
- **Must stay on-device**: the popup's resource-id reachability and the IME typing inside the rename step.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the spec (`AGENDA-06.yaml`) says the whole path is what catches "Save spins forever" and a list-level delete that mis-handles the confirm; a single compose test can walk the whole path in one JVM run instead of 146 s of device time.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `agenda/07-copy-to-profile.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: the copy lands in the target profile's namespace with its own id and does not overwrite the source; the list shows the copy; the UI reports "Copied to Personal".
- **Production boundary**: `SavedAgendaListIntent.CopyToProfile(viewId, targetProfileId)` → `SavedAgendaListViewModel` (`.../presentation/viewmodel/SavedAgendaListViewModel.kt:48,96`) → `SavedAgendaViewFactory.duplicateForProfile` → `SavedAgendaViewsRepositoryImpl.duplicateForProfile` (`.../feature/agenda/data/SavedAgendaViewsRepositoryImpl.kt:95`); the sheet is `SavedAgendaListScreen.kt:306` (`saved_agenda_copy_to_profile_sheet`), the message `SavedAgendaListScreen.kt:92`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/agenda/SavedAgendaListViewModelTest.kt` (the `CopyToProfile` intent and the copy) and `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/agenda/SavedAgendaViewsRepositoryImplTest.kt` (`duplicateForProfile_writesTheCopyIntoTheTargetProfileNamespace`, `duplicateForProfile_doesNotRestampTheCopyIntoTheSourceNamespace`). The *UI* half (menu row → sheet → profile item → snackbar) has no owner — a desktop compose test would be the natural place.
- **Must stay on-device**: the profile-picker sheet presentation on a phone, the snackbar's rendering, and the IME typing of the view's name.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the MR-5 bug (the copy re-stamped with the *current* scoped user, landing in the wrong namespace) is invisible in the UI unless you inspect the DB — exactly the class of defect a repository/ViewModel test catches and a snackbar assertion does not.
- **Confidence**: medium (the UI half is currently unowned; the copy semantics are owned).

### `agenda/10-overflow-menu.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: the two infrequent actions are not on the bar, are in the overflow menu, carry a visible label, and the multi-date row still opens the multi-date picker; cancelling closes the picker and leaves the agenda as it was.
- **Production boundary**: `AgendaContent.kt` (the `DropdownMenu` and its rows, `AgendaIntent.CreateOnDates`), `AgendaTopBarIcons`/`AgendaScreen`, and `MultiDatePicker` (sheet) with `TestTags.MultiDatePicker.*`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/agenda/AgendaTopBarOverflowFlowTest.kt` — `the_infrequent_actions_are_reachable_from_a_labeled_overflow_menu` and `the_multi_date_row_still_opens_the_date_picker` assert the same contract; `AgendaTopBarStringsResourceTest` owns the wording.
- **Must stay on-device**: the popup-window resource ids — the menu is its own window and only `mapTestTagsAsResourceIds` makes its rows addressable by UiAutomator; no device test covers this today (grep in `androidApp/src/androidTest` and `shared/src/androidHostTest` for the agenda menu tags found nothing), so the guarantee would be *lost* if the flow were simply dropped — it should move to a device instrumented test instead.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: an unlabelled glyph row is exactly the defect the overflow was meant to fix, and it is a pure semantics-tree claim.
- **Confidence**: high for the behaviour half; the resource-id half has no owner today.

### `negative/01-empty-inbox.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: the Inbox agenda with no tasks renders the shared empty state ("No tasks") rather than a blank list.
- **Production boundary**: `AgendaContent` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/agenda/presentation/screen/AgendaContent.kt`) rendering the shared `EmptyState` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../core/ui/components/EmptyState.kt`); the agenda state machine in `AgendaViewModel`/`AgendaUiState`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/DesktopAppBootTest.kt` already asserts "No tasks" on the boot agenda (Today tab); the Inbox tab's own empty state is the same shared composable. `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../core/ui/components/EmptyStateTest.kt` owns the empty-state component itself.
- **Must stay on-device**: `tasks_fab` — the tag is applied only in `shared/src/androidMain/.../AndroidShellNav3.kt:189`, and finding it as a resource id is an Android-shell property (the desktop shell's FAB is addressed by content description).
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a blank agenda is indistinguishable from a failed load for a user; the text is a shared-UI contract.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `negative/05-empty-search.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only**: an empty query shows the idle prompt; a non-matching query shows the no-results state (and not the idle prompt).
- **Production boundary**: the pure function `searchContent(query, results, isSearching)` in `shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchContent.kt`, consumed by `SearchContent` (`.../feature/search/SearchContent.kt`) and the `search_empty_placeholder` / `search_empty_results` tags.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchContentTest.kt` (`@Tag("fast")`) already owns the decision — `an empty query shows the idle prompt`, `a query that matched nothing shows the no-results state`, `a query that matched nothing does not show the idle prompt`. Its own header says the *composable that draws it has no Compose test harness in this module*, which is the remaining gap: nothing asserts that `search_empty_placeholder` and `search_empty_results` are the tags the screen actually shows.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing of the query (the flow types `zzzznomatchzzzz` through the platform keyboard) and the search entry point from the Android menu (`menu_quick_search`).
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: "the screen shows the idle prompt after a failed query" is the defect the two states exist to prevent, and it is a one-line decision.
- **Confidence**: high for the split. Note: this flow **fails in every sampled run** and has no passing sample, so which of its two assertions currently holds on a device is unverified (see Uncertainties).

### `negative/06-empty-tags.yaml` — split

The natural home is a desktop compose test beside `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tags/` (where `TagsRenameUiTest.kt` and `TagsUndoFabClearanceTest.kt` already live) or a `shared/src/jvmTest` test on the tags screen state.

- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### Task sub-features: checklist, logbook, subtasks

### `tasks/checklist/TASK-CHECK-01.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions** (what would move to a cheaper tier): the checklist section is drawn on the task screen even when the task has no checklist items; tapping the section header opens it and reveals the inline add-row (`checklist_inline_add_input`); typing there and pressing `checklist_inline_add_button` adds the item **without** any sheet opening; the item is listed as `checklist_item_<slug>` with a **real checkbox** (`checklist_item_checkbox_<slug>`); tapping that checkbox flips it to `checked: true`; the section's count reflects the finished items.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary** (`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/`): `feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskChildrenSlot.kt` — the intents `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.AddChecklistItem` (→ `addChecklistItem`, line 124, `checklistRepository.addItem(taskId.value, title.trim())`), `.ToggleChecklistItem` (→ `toggleChecklistItem`, line 133, `checklistRepository.toggleItem(taskId.value, intent.item.id, "user")`), `.DeleteChecklistItem` (→ line 146) and `.MoveChecklistItem`; the intent data classes live in `feature/tasks/domain/state/TaskDetailIntent.kt`; the write layer is `feature/checklist/components/ChecklistItemRow.kt` (row + `checklistItemCheckbox`/`checklistItemDelete` tags) and `feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/` for the section; the section's rendering is `ChecklistSection` with `TestTags.TASK_CHECKLIST_SECTION` / `CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_INPUT` / `CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_BUTTON`.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for replacement**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/TaskItemListScenarioTest.kt` (class `TaskItemListScenarioTest`, `@Tag("slow")` + `@Tag("slow:compose")`, `@DisplayName("TASK-ITEM-01 the task screen lists both kinds of item with checkboxes")`). It already opens `TASK_CHECKLIST_SECTION`, ticks `TestTags.checklistItemCheckbox(...)` and awaits the checked state, adds an item through `CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_INPUT`/`CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_BUTTON`, awaits the new `checklistItem`, and asserts `TestTags.CHECKLIST_SHEET` does **not** exist — i.e. strictly more than the Maestro flow asserts, including the count the flow deliberately skips. `TaskDetailEmptyChildSectionsTest.kt` is the companion for the empty-state half.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device**: (a) the FAB→editor path on the Android shell — `tasks_fab` is applied only in `shared/src/androidMain/.../AndroidShellNav3.kt`, the desktop shell's FAB has no such tag; (b) the real IME keeping `checklist_inline_add_input` on screen (the flow's UXF-03/#2874 `assertVisible`); (c) the resource-id layer — `id:` selectors resolve to UIAutomator ids only because `shared/src/androidMain/.../App.kt` sets `testTagsAsResourceId = true` at the root, and the desktop actual of `mapTestTagsAsResourceIds()` is a no-op.
- **Why a headless test would catch a real regression**: the checklist used to be unreachable from the task screen (the editor passed `checklist = null`, so the only way in was the FirstRunSection's "Add checklist" chip — the flow's own header says so). "The section hides itself when empty" is exactly a shared-UI defect that a semantics-tree assertion in seconds catches, whereas the Maestro flow needs 78 s of device time, creates a task per run, and currently fails in 2 of 5 sampled runs.
- **Confidence**: high for the split. The behaviour half is already owned by a desktop twin; the IME half is already owned by `TaskDetailImeInsetsTest`; what would genuinely be lost is the *Android-shell FAB → editor* path, which no device test covers either — that needs an explicit owner (an `androidApp/src/androidTest` case) rather than the whole 78 s flow.

### `tasks/logbook/TASK-LOG-01.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: the `+ Ещё` sheet offers "Logbook" for a task that has no logbook entries, and picking it puts the logbook section on the screen; the logbook's "+" opens a note editor **attached to that task**; the first save writes the note with the task attachment; after leaving the editor and reopening the task from scratch, the logbook lists the note under the typed title (`logbook_note_by_title_<slug>`).
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary** (`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/`): `feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/LogbookSection.kt` — `LOGBOOK_SECTION` header, the `LOGBOOK_ADD_NOTE_BUTTON` `IconButton` (line 113) whose `onClick` calls `onAddNote(currentTaskId)`, and the note row tagged `TestTags.logbookNoteByTitle(title)` (line 219); `feature/tasks/presentation/screen/TaskDetailContent.kt` — `propertiesWithContent` adds `TaskOptionalProperty.Logbook` **only** when `ui.logbookEntries.isNotEmpty()` (line 477) and renders `LogbookSection(… onAddNote = { navigator.openCreateNote(it) })` (lines 1094-1098); `feature/tasks/presentation/nav/TasksNavigator.kt` — `openCreateNote(taskId)` → `onExitGraph(AppDestination.NotesGraph(AppDestination.NotesStartRoute.EditorForTask(taskId)))` (line 58); `feature/notes/presentation/viewmodel/NoteEditor.kt` — `createNoteForTask(taskId)` keeps the task on the draft and writes nothing, and `writeNote(draft)` calls `repo.createForTask(id, taskId, title, …)` for a new task-bound draft (~line 492).
- **Exact existing test file suitable for replacement**: the *rule* is already owned by `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/notes/NotesRepositoryLogbookAttachmentTest.kt` (class `NotesRepositoryLogbookAttachmentTest`, `@Tag("slow")` + `@Tag("slow:data")`, real in-memory SQLite) — `createForTask writes the attachment onto the note`, `the attached note is in the task's logbook right after it is created`, `a content save does not drop the attachment — the editor's autosave path`. The *UI seam* has no owner: `LogbookSection.kt` is rendered by `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/tasks/TaskDetailEmptyChildSectionsTest.kt` only as far as `awaitTag(TestTags.LOGBOOK_SECTION)` after `clickTag(TestTags.moreParameterRow("Logbook"))` (lines 169-170) — it never taps `LOGBOOK_ADD_NOTE_BUTTON` and never asserts a note row. The natural replacement target is that same desktop flow file, extended with: click `LOGBOOK_ADD_NOTE_BUTTON` → the notes graph's editor opens (`NOTE_EDITOR_TITLE_INPUT`) → type a title → save → reopen the task → the logbook shows `logbookNoteByTitle(title)`.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device**: the cross-graph exit (the "+" leaves the tasks nested graph for the app-level notes graph, so `back` lands on the agenda) and the re-entry from the Inbox tab — both are properties of the Android Nav3 shell and of the seeded deep link; the real IME in the note title field; the `note_editor_saved_indicator` pill and the resource-id layer.
- **Why a headless test would catch a real regression**: the bug the flow guards is a *data* bug — Room's `@Upsert` replacing every column on the first save, so `task_id` went NULL and the note fell out of the logbook. No UI assertion can see that; only a read-back of the stored note can. The headless owners already read it back, so the device round-trip adds cost without adding signal — except that the *UI* half (the "+" actually hands the task id to the editor, and the saved note is rendered in the task's logbook under the typed title) is currently unowned and would be caught in seconds by a desktop compose test.
- **Confidence**: medium-high. The data rule is triple-owned; the UI seam is unowned; the graph-exit guarantee has no cheaper tier and would be lost unless the flow is kept for that half.

### `tasks/logbook/TASK-LOG-02.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: the logbook section, when revealed from the `+ Ещё` sheet on a task with no entries, renders the **empty state** (`logbook_empty`) rather than a blank card; opening the logbook's "+" and leaving the editor without typing leaves the logbook exactly as it was — still `logbook_empty`, no note row written.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary**: `feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/LogbookSection.kt` lines 128-136 — `if (entries.isEmpty()) Box(Modifier.testTag(TestTags.LOGBOOK_EMPTY)) { EmptySectionHint(text = "No notes or time entries yet. Add a note to start the log.", modifier = Modifier.testTag(TestTags.LOGBOOK_EMPTY_HINT)) }`; the action that must not write is the autosave lambda in `feature/notes/presentation/viewmodel/NoteEditor.kt:102-117` — `if (draft.isNew && !draft.isDirty) Unit` — and `writeNote` (same file, ~483-506) creating via `repo.createForTask` only when `draft.isNew && draft.taskId != null`, otherwise `createWithContent`.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for replacement**: the *rule* is already owned by `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/notes/presentation/viewmodel/NoteEditorLogbookAttachmentTest.kt` (class `NoteEditorLogbookAttachmentTest`, `@Tag("fast")`) — `opening the logbook's editor writes nothing` asserts "an untouched draft must not be written", and `the draft keeps the task while the note is still unwritten`. The *empty-state card* has **no** owner: `TestTags.LOGBOOK_EMPTY` / `LOGBOOK_EMPTY_HINT` are referenced by no test in the tree (grep over `shared/src`, `desktopApp/src`, `androidApp/src/androidTest` finds them only in `LogbookSection.kt` and `TestTags.kt`). The replacement target is `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/TaskDetailEmptyChildSectionsTest.kt`, which already reveals the logbook through `moreParameterRow("Logbook")` and awaits `LOGBOOK_SECTION` — one `awaitTag(TestTags.LOGBOOK_EMPTY)` away.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device**: the cross-graph exit and the `back`-lands-on-the-agenda behaviour (the flow itself carries a conditional second `back` because "where 'back' lands depends on the graph it was pushed on"), the seeded deep link, and the resource-id layer. The real IME is *not* involved in this flow (nothing is typed).
- **Why a headless test would catch a real regression**: "the editor creates the note on open, so an abandoned editor leaves an empty row" was the actual defect — and it is a one-line condition in the autosave guard that a unit test pins exactly. The *visual* half (the logbook renders a different card when it holds something) is a shared-UI claim that is currently asserted by nothing at all: a regression that drops `LOGBOOK_EMPTY` would leave a user staring at a card with a title and a button and no explanation, and no tier would notice.
- **Confidence**: high for the behaviour split. Note the flow **failed on 2026-11-21**; its three passing samples have a 64 s median (the slowest was 74 s), and with no emulator the failing step is unknown (see Uncertainties).

### `tasks/subtasks/TASK-SUB-01.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: the subtasks section is drawn on the task screen even when the task has no subtasks; tapping its header opens it and reveals the inline add-row (`subtask_add_input`); submitting adds the subtask **as a child of this task**; the subtask row carries a **real checkbox** (`subtask_checkbox_<slug>`) whose tap flips `checked: true`; the delete control (`subtask_delete_<slug>`) removes the row from the list.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary** (`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/`): `feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskChildrenSlot.kt` — the intents `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.AddSubtask` (→ `addSubtask`, line 205: `core.createTask(CreateTaskInput(title = title.trim(), parentTaskId = parent.id, followsUp = setOf(parent.id)))`), `.ToggleSubtask` (→ `toggleSubtask`, line 224: `core.taskRepo.complete(subtask.id, force)` / `core.completion.reopen(subtask.id)`), `.DeleteSubtask` (→ `deleteSubtask`, line 267: `core.taskRepo.softDelete(subtask.id)` + `onUndoDeleteSubtask`), `.RestoreSubtask` and `.MoveSubtask`; the intent data classes in `feature/tasks/domain/state/TaskDetailIntent.kt`; the create door in `feature/tasks/domain/usecase/CreateTask.kt` (`CreateTaskUseCase` → `TaskDomain.createInput`, `parentTaskId` param in `feature/tasks/domain/TaskDomain.kt:53/82/121`); the row in `feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/SubtasksSection.kt` (`SUBTASK_ADD_INPUT` line 168, `SUBTASK_ADD_BUTTON` line 192) and `feature/checklist/components/ChecklistItemRow.kt` (`subtaskDelete` tag line 69).
- **Exact existing test file suitable for replacement**: the *coordinator* half is already owned by `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskChildrenSlotTest.kt` (class `TaskChildrenSlotTest`, `@Tag("fast")`) — `add subtask parents it to the current task` asserts `parentTaskId == TaskId("t1")`, `followsUp == setOf(TaskId("t1"))` and `blockedBy.isEmpty()`, plus `delete subtask soft-deletes it`, `deleting a subtask offers an undo`, `an unblocked subtask closes without a question` and `a subtask with an unfinished prerequisite is not closed and asks instead`; the *use-case door* is owned by `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/domain/usecase/CreateTaskAttributeCarryOverTest.kt` (class `CreateTaskAttributeCarryOverTest`, `@Tag("fast")`), whose header names the exact #171 defect class; the *UI* half lives in `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/TaskDetailEmptyChildSectionsTest.kt` (adds a subtask through `SUBTASK_ADD_INPUT`/`SUBTASK_ADD_BUTTON`, awaits "Subtasks (0/1)") and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/ChecklistAndSubtasksSectionUiTest.kt` ("a subtask's title opens it and its checkbox completes it"). The **delete** action is the gap: `TestTags.subtaskDelete(title)` is asserted by no test in the tree.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device**: the debug-seed deep link (`helpers/seed.yaml` → `DebugSeedActivity`) and the seeded row landing on the Today tab (`due=today`); the real IME keeping `subtask_add_input` on screen — already owned by `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/TaskDetailImeInsetsTest.kt` (`SUBTASK_ADD_INPUT` with `SUBTASKS_SECTION` as the scroll anchor); the resource-id layer.
- **Why a headless test would catch a real regression**: #171 was a *silent* failure — the use case accepted `parentTaskId` and dropped it, so "add subtask" produced a top-level task that appeared nowhere near its parent and nowhere in the agenda. Nothing in the UI says "wrong"; only a read-back of the created task's `parentTaskId` does, and `TaskChildrenSlotTest.add subtask parents it to the current task` does exactly that. The remaining unowned claim is the delete control: a subtask that is not removed from the section (or that is hard-deleted instead of soft-deleted, breaking the undo) is a coordinator defect the device flow currently is the only owner of.
- **Confidence**: high for the split (add/toggle/parenting are already owned twice); medium for the delete half, which is a cheap new desktop compose assertion rather than an existing owner.

### Tasks

Each candidate names: the **behaviour-only** Maestro assertions, the production boundary they
exercise, the **exact existing test file** that can carry the replacement, the platform
assertions that must stay on-device, and why a headless test would catch the regression that
matters.

### C1 — `03-due-date-cancel.yaml` → replace the sheet half (confidence: high)

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: «Сегодня» chip tapped → `task_scheduling_cancel`
  tapped → `task_scheduling_sheet` gone → `task_editor_due_date_row_placeholder` still
  visible (nothing written). The claim "a dismissed sheet writes nothing" is the whole flow.
- **Production boundary**: `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.SetSchedule` (`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/domain/state/TaskDetailIntent.kt`) → `TaskDetailCoordinator.onIntent` (`…/feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/TaskDetailCoordinator.kt`) → `TaskEntitySlot` (`…/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskEntitySlot.kt`), whose scheduling half is `TaskEntitySlotScheduleTest`'s subject.
- **Existing test file for the replacement**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/TaskSchedulingSheetTest.kt` — it already holds `cancelling writes nothing at all`, i.e. the exact rule. For the *row-level* residue ("the editor row still shows the placeholder after a cancelled sheet"), the editor-level file `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/SetDueDateFlowTest.kt` is the right host (it already drives the real editor by tapping a row and asserting the placeholder/labels).
- **Must stay on-device**: nothing in this flow's claims. The sheet is a `ModalBottomSheet`; per `Maestro/CONVENTIONS.md` and `SheetReachabilityTest`, its content is reachable from the JVM tier through the scene's roots.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression that matters**: the defect class is "the sheet wrote on dismissal" — a data write, not a rendering. A JVM test fails in seconds against the real `TaskEntitySlot` write path, where a device run needs a sheet, a chip, a cancel and a read-back. Confidence **high**.

### C2 — `01-set-priority.yaml` → split (confidence: medium)

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: tap the priority row → the sheet shows "No priority"
  → tap "High priority" → the sheet closes → the row reads "High priority" and no longer
  "No priority".
- **Production boundary**: `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.SetPriority` → `TaskDetailCoordinator` →
  `TaskEntitySlot` (the write is asserted by `S/feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskEntitySlotTest.kt`, `set priority writes through to the repository`).
- **Existing test file for the replacement**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/SetPriorityFlowTest.kt` — it is already labelled a desktop mirror of this exact flow and already asserts the relabel (`choosing_high_updates_the_row_label`), the row's addressability and all five options' ids. **The duplicated half needs no new test at all.**
- **Must stay on-device**: the *addressability* fact this flow records — `priority_option_*` testTags do not reach the Android accessibility tree from inside the sheet's `Popup` window, so the option must be reached by its label. That is a fact about the Android resource-id layer; keep it as a one-line device check or as a note in `Maestro/CONVENTIONS.md`, not as a 41 s flow.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression that matters**: the regression the flow was written for (`docs/decisions/2026-09-29-editor-row-onclick-noop-default.md`) was a **dead tap** — the row's `onClick` defaulted to an empty lambda. `SetPriorityFlowTest.choosing_high_updates_the_row_label` fails on exactly that defect in seconds. Confidence **medium** (the device residue is real but small, and the label tap is locale-fragile — the same suite notes the device runs in Russian).

### C3 — `02-set-due-date.yaml` → split (confidence: medium)

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: tap the due row → the sheet opens → «Сегодня» →
  confirm → the sheet closes → the row's label is visible and the placeholder is gone.
- **Production boundary**: `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.SetSchedule` → `TaskDetailCoordinator` →
  `TaskEntitySlot` (scheduling). Writes are asserted in `S/…/slot/TaskEntitySlotScheduleTest.kt`
  (`clear due date writes null`, `the whole schedule is written in one go`).
- **Existing test file for the replacement**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/presentation/components/detail/TaskSchedulingSheetTest.kt` for the sheet ("a tapped day is the date the sheet hands over"), and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/SetDueDateFlowTest.kt` for the editor row (it already asserts the row's tag and the placeholder-vs-label state).
- **Must stay on-device**: the *row → sheet* wiring on Android and the fact that the sheet's own window has to be addressed from the outside — thin, and the sheet's ids do reach the tree (the flow uses `task_scheduling_sheet`, `task_scheduling_today_chip`, `task_scheduling_confirm` by id).
- **Why a headless test catches the regression that matters**: "confirm writes the date and the row stops reading the placeholder" is a state transition across a coordinator hop; `SetDueDateFlowTest` already fails on it without a device. Confidence **medium**.

### C4 — `04-delete.yaml` → split, shrink to the list-level claim (confidence: medium)

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: overflow menu → Delete → back → the agenda is visible →
  `task_item_buy_milk` is gone. The "the tap routed to *this* task" assertion (title "Buy
  milk" on the field) is duplicated by `D/feature/flows/agenda/OpenTaskFromAgendaFlowTest.kt`.
- **Production boundary**: `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.Delete` → `TaskDetailCoordinator` →
  `TaskLifecycleSlot` (`S/…/slot/TaskLifecycleSlotTest.kt`: `delete soft-deletes and offers undo`).
  The list-level absence is `AgendaIntent.TaskDeleteClicked` → `AgendaViewModel`.
- **Existing test file for the replacement**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/TaskDetailOverflowActionsTest.kt` already asserts the delete write and the undo offer; the residue ("the row is gone from the agenda") fits `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/TaskRowFlowTest.kt` / `AgendaTabDefinitionFlowTest.kt`, which already own the agenda's row membership.
- **Must stay on-device**: the Android shell's own overflow bar and its `overflow_delete` resource-id — owned by `A/TaskDetailOverflowActionsTest.kt` (`one_tap_on_delete_deletes_the_task_and_offers_the_delete_undo`), so the flow need not repeat it.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression that matters**: "the row disappeared from the list" is an agenda-state regression (the row is still in the UI because the list did not re-read the repository) — exactly what a JVM agenda test catches in seconds. Confidence **medium**; note this is a `smoke` flow, so shrinking it changes PR-path cost.

### C5 — `09-rename-empty.yaml` → replace-candidate (confidence: high)

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: clearing the title → the editor stays open → back → the
  row still reads "RenameMe" (the task was not renamed to a blank/"Untitled").
- **Production boundary**: the rule is `TaskTitleValidation` (`shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/domain/state/TaskTitleValidation.kt`); the write refusal is `TaskDraftSlot` (`…/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskDraftSlot.kt`).
- **Existing test file for the replacement**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/domain/state/TaskTitleValidationTest.kt` (the rule), `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskDraftSlotTest.kt` (`a blank title is refused and the draft returns to the stored one`), and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/tasks/CreateTaskFlowTest.kt` (the editor's validation message and the disabled save).
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME's **select-all** (long-press → selection → erase) and the platform keyboard's erase of a Compose `BasicTextField`. That is the only device-specific claim, and it is a low-value one.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression that matters**: "an empty title is refused and the stored title survives" is a domain rule; the flow is red in all 5 sampled runs, so today the rule is protected only by the JVM tier anyway. Confidence **high**.

### C6 — `14-prefill-from-today.yaml` / `15-prefill-from-inbox.yaml` / `19-prefill-from-upcoming.yaml` → replace-candidate (confidence: high for the rule, medium for the scenario bookkeeping)

- **Why a headless test catches the regression that matters**: the prefill is a pure function of the
