### Auth, backup, attachments, archive

### `archive/02-bulk-archive-confirm.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: the bulk action lives in the overflow, not in the content area; the old full-width button is gone; confirming is required before anything is written; **Cancel writes nothing**; Confirm moves exactly one completed task to the archive; the report names the count ("Moved 1 task to archive"); the archived row is then listed.
- **Production boundary**: `ArchiveIntent` → `ArchiveViewModel.archiveCompletedTasks()` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/archive/ArchiveViewModel.kt:120`), which emits `ArchiveUiEvent.Archived("Moved $count $noun to archive")` (`ArchiveViewModel.kt:133`); the screen is `ArchiveScreen` with `TestTags.ARCHIVE_EMPTY` / `ARCHIVE_OVERFLOW` / `ARCHIVE_BULK_ACTION`; the write is `TaskRepository.softDelete`.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/archive/ArchiveBulkActionFlowTest.kt` — `the_bulk_archive_confirms_before_writing` already asserts the overflow path, the confirm dialog, "Cancel must not archive anything" (via `archivedAt(koin)` reading `archivedAt` off the repository), the row appearing and the write being real; plus `the_confirmation_does_not_reappear_after_eligibility_returns` and `the_action_is_disabled_without_eligible_tasks`. The wording is owned by `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/archive/ArchiveViewModelTest.kt` (`a single archived task is reported in the singular`).
- **Must stay on-device**: the long-press context-menu sheet (`task_context_menu_sheet`) — a real gesture with a real popup; the confirm dialog's `dialog_confirm` resource id and the result dialog's modal window covering the archive list; the overflow popup's resource ids. The last two are already reachable as resource ids elsewhere in the device tier (`CreateTaskFlowTest`, `EditorBackGuardTest` use `dialog_confirm`), so a device owner for the archive overflow would be a small addition — but it does not exist today.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: the failure mode this scenario exists for is a bulk action that archives without confirmation or archives on Cancel. A UI-only assertion ("the row moved") can pass while the wrong rows moved; the desktop twin reads the repository and checks the count, which is the difference between a test and a screenshot.
- **Confidence**: high for the replacement of the behaviour; the platform residue (long-press sheet, overflow popup ids) needs a new device owner.

### `archive/01-restore.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: archiving through the detail overflow removes the task from the live list; the archived task is listed on the Archive screen; opening it and choosing "Restore" removes it from the Archive list and puts it back in Inbox.
- **Production boundary**: `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.Archive` / `TaskDetailIntent.Domain.Unarchive` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/tasks/domain/state/TaskDetailIntent.kt:247,258`) → `TaskLifecycleSlot.archive()` / `.unarchive()` (`.../feature/tasks/presentation/viewmodel/slot/TaskLifecycleSlot.kt:72,156`) → `TaskRepository.softDelete` / `.restore`; the Archive list is fed by `ArchiveRepository`.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: the archive half is already owned twice — `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/tasks/TaskDetailOverflowActionsTest.kt` (`one_tap_opens_the_menu_and_one_tap_on_archive_archives_the_task`) and the device twin `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/TaskDetailOverflowActionsTest.kt`, which reads the write back. The **restore half has no owner**: `TaskLifecycleSlot.unarchive()` (the `Восстановить` row, gated on the trashed state) is asserted nowhere I found, and the device test's header says so explicitly ("restores the task is #2723's, and that defect is open"). A `shared/src/jvmTest` test on `TaskLifecycleSlot` with the fake repository would own it in seconds.
- **Must stay on-device**: the detail overflow's popup resource ids (`overflow_archive` / `overflow_restore`), and the Archive screen being a *menu destination* on Android (a tab on desktop) — the entry path differs per platform.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: a restore that silently fails leaves the user's task in the trash with the UI saying "Task restored"; that is a data-loss-shaped defect, and it is exactly what issue #2723 is about.
- **Confidence**: high for the restore half (it is unowned and cheap); medium for the overall split, because the flow carries no `scenario:` tag and `ARCHIVE-01` shows up as a hole in `traceability validate`.

### `attachments/03-empty-url-rejected.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: with an empty URL field, no attachment is added and the sheet still shows "No attachments yet"; the URL field is still there.
- **Production boundary**: the guard itself, `AttachmentsSheet.kt:142,153` — `if (url.isNotBlank())` and `enabled = urlState.text.isNotBlank()`; below it, `AttachmentsIntent.AddUrl` → `AttachmentsViewModel.addUrl` → `AttachmentRepositoryImpl.addUrlAttachment`, which validates with `AttachmentDomain.validateUrl(url).getOrThrow()` (`core/attachments/AttachmentRepository.kt:206`).
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: **none exists for `ATTACH-03`** — `AttachmentDomainTest.validateUrl*` (`shared/src/commonTest/.../core/attachments/`) owns the *syntax* rule, but the empty-string case is blocked by a Compose-level `enabled` flag, not by the domain. The natural owner is a desktop compose test beside `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/attachments/AttachmentsSheetReachabilityFlowTest.kt`, which already mounts the task detail and opens the sheet.
- **Must stay on-device**: the sheet as a modal bottom sheet and the fact that a disabled button is still on screen (the flow's own note).
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: the guard is one boolean in one composable; if it is dropped, an empty URL reaches `validateUrl` and the user gets an error dialog instead of a disabled button — a behaviour change a device test sees only as "no attachment appeared", indistinguishable from a tap that did not land.
- **Confidence**: high. This is the best value-per-second candidate in the area: 35 s of device time for a one-line rule.

### `attachments/01-add-url.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: a URL attachment added from the sheet appears on the task, rendering both the title and the URL.
- **Production boundary**: `AttachmentsIntent.AddUrl(taskId, url, title)` → `AttachmentsViewModel.addUrl` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/attachments/AttachmentsViewModel.kt:69`) → `AttachmentRepositoryImpl.addUrlAttachment` (validates the URL, generates the id, writes the Room row) → the section's tile list.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: **none for `ATTACH-01`**. `AttachmentsViewModelTest` (`shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/attachments/`) owns only delete/undo; `AttachmentsSheetReachabilityFlowTest` (desktop) owns the sheet's reachability, not the add path. A `shared/src/jvmTest` test on `AttachmentsViewModel` with the fake repository, or a desktop compose test that mounts the sheet, would own the write + the rendering.
- **Must stay on-device**: the `+ Ещё` more-sheet as the way in (its rule — "the sheet offers exactly the sections that are *not* on the screen" — is an Android state-dependent behaviour), the IME, and the scroll/reveal choreography that the helper scripts encode.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: an attachment that is written but not rendered (or rendered but not written) is the classic split-brain defect; the repository write and the tile's rendering are two different facts and a device text assertion can only see the second.
- **Confidence**: medium — the split is clean, but the flow is red in CI and its red-ness is engine-sensitive (see Uncertainties), so the carve-up should wait for the triage.

### `attachments/02-delete.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: a URL attachment appears on the task; tapping its delete affordance removes it from the list.
- **Production boundary**: `AttachmentsIntent.Delete(attachmentId)` → `AttachmentsViewModel.delete` (`AttachmentsViewModel.kt:81`) → `AttachmentRepository.delete` → on success `AttachmentsUiEvent.UndoDelete`; `AttachmentsIntent.Restore` → `AttachmentsViewModel.restore`.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/attachments/AttachmentsViewModelTest.kt` — `delete emits undo event and restore brings attachment back` already owns the coordinator rule; `AttachmentStorageTest.deleteFileRemovesFile` owns the file removal. What is missing is only the tile's disappearance after the delete.
- **Must stay on-device**: the delete affordance is a **content description** ("Delete attachment") because the attachment id is a UUID and there is no stable tag — a device-only addressing property; the sheet.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: delete-without-undo loses work; the undo event is already owned, and the UI half is the part that can silently stop offering it.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `attachments/04-persistence.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: after an app restart, the attachment is still on the task; the section arrives collapsed and must be opened before its rows are visible.
- **Production boundary**: the write is `AttachmentRepositoryImpl.addUrlAttachment` → `AttachmentDao.upsert` (Room); the read is `repository.watchByTask(taskId)` (`AttachmentsViewModel.kt:56-57`); the reopen/expand state is `TaskDetailContent`'s section state.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: **none for `ATTACH-04`**. `AttachmentStorageTest` (`shared/src/commonTest/.../core/attachments/`) is a fake filesystem, not Room; no real round trip for attachment rows was found in the tree. The natural owner is a `shared/src/jvmTest` test against a real Room database (the pattern `MigrationRoundTripSupport` owns) or a desktop compose test over the fake repository.
- **Must stay on-device**: a real process kill + relaunch against a real Room file on device storage.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: an attachment row that is written but not re-readable (a missing Room entity, a userId-scoped `WHERE` clause, a `deletedAt` written by mistake) is invisible in a UI assertion that only looks at the current session — the row was there a second ago and is not there now.
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the headless half; the flow is red in CI, so the split should follow the triage.

### `attachments/05-renders-correctly.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: a URL attachment renders its title, its URL text, and a delete affordance; the add-file row is present on the section.
- **Production boundary**: the tile composable in the attachments section (the URL type's rendering, `AttachmentType.Url`) and `TestTags.ATTACHMENTS_ADD_FILE_ROW`.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: **none for `ATTACH-05`** — the rendering of a URL attachment is asserted nowhere headless. A desktop compose test (the section, not the sheet) would own it.
- **Must stay on-device**: the "Delete attachment" content description; the add-file row as the platform file-picker entry.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: a URL attachment drawn with the file icon instead of the link icon, or without its title, is a pure rendering defect that a text-only device assertion partly misses (the flow asserts a URL *text* and a title text, but the icon is only implied by a comment).
- **Confidence**: medium.

### `auth/02-signin-wrong-password.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: a rejected sign-in is presented as the shared error dialog; the email address stays in the form (it is not cleared on error).
- **Production boundary**: `AuthIntent.SignIn` → `AuthViewModel.signIn` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/auth/AuthViewModel.kt:225`) → `AuthUiEvent.Error` → `AuthUiEventPresentationTest`'s mapping (an error becomes the error dialog); the "email stays" claim is the login screen's text-field state.

- **Must stay on-device**: the E2E stub (`TestAuthStubs.kt`) and its `run-as` flag file (`signin_failure`), the real IME, the error dialog's own window.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: the sign-in form clearing the address on failure is the classic account-recovery annoyance; it is a screen-state rule with no device-specific content.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `auth/03-signup-toggle.yaml` / `auth/06-signup-unconfirmed.yaml` — split (two flows, one boundary)

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions** (`03`): the form starts in sign-in mode; the toggle switches it to sign-up and the labels change; submitting an unconfirmed sign-up does not let the user in (`nav_tab_today` is not visible). (`06`): the "check your mail" headline and the address are shown; **no error dialog** — the outcome is a neutral notice.
- **Production boundary**: `AuthIntent.SignUp` → `AuthViewModel.signUp` (`AuthViewModel.kt:246`) → `AuthUiState.AwaitingEmailConfirmation` → `AuthUiEvent.Message` (a neutral notice) vs `AuthUiEvent.Error` (a dialog) — the two presentations are separated by `AuthUiEventPresentationTest`.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/auth/AuthViewModelTest.kt` — `a sign-up awaiting confirmation does not enter the application`, `... emits no navigation`, `... does not seed`, `... says what to do, and not as an error`; plus `AuthUiEventPresentationTest.a neutral notice is presented as a snackbar, never as an error`. **Neither `AUTH-SIGNUP-01` nor `AUTH-SIGNUP-02` has a carrier.**
- **Must stay on-device**: the stub's `unconfirmed_signup` flag file, the IME, and the mode-toggle button's own resource id. The toggle *labels* ("Sign Up", "Already have an account? Sign In") are copy — a screen-level (desktop compose) test, not a ViewModel test.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: dressing a successful sign-up as an error makes people retype an address that is already registered — the exact harm `AuthUiEvent.Message` was introduced to prevent. The ViewModel tier already pins it.
- **Confidence**: high for the behaviour half.

### `auth/07-account-attach.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: the attach screen shows email, password and submit; the offline-data disposition must be answered before the form can be submitted; a successful attach ends on the agenda home.
- **Production boundary**: `AuthIntent.AttachAccount` → `AuthViewModel.attachAccount` (`AuthViewModel.kt:311`) → `LocalAccountClaimer.claim(email, password, disposition)` (`feature/auth/LocalAccountClaimer.kt:69`) → on `ClaimStep.Done`, `AuthUiEvent.NavigateToHome`; the disposition is `LoginForm.disposition` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/auth/LoginFormState.kt`), rendered by `AttachAccountScreen.kt:297` (`submitEnabled = disposition != null`).
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/auth/AuthViewModelTest.kt` — `a successful AttachAccount transitions session and navigates home`, `both dispositions reach the repository rather than one being assumed`, `a failed AttachAccount reports an error and keeps the local session`, `a failed AttachAccount does not navigate home`; and `LocalAccountClaimerTest` (the data movement itself). **No class carries `AUTH-ATTACH-01`.**
- **Must stay on-device**: the path into the screen (settings bottom sheet → Account tab → the anonymous-only "Attach account" button) and the **soft keyboard covering the submit button** — the flow's own `hideKeyboard` calls are the proof that this is a device layout property.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: "neither disposition is a default" (REQ-UA-005) is a data-loss rule: an attach that defaults to Discard erases the user's offline data. `AuthViewModelTest.both dispositions reach the repository rather than one being assumed` already pins it.
- **Confidence**: high.

### `auth/10-attach-account-validation-error.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: an empty submission does not leave the attach screen; a password that is too short is refused with the shared error dialog.
- **Production boundary**: `LocalAccountClaimer` validates before it touches anything (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/auth/LocalAccountClaimer.kt:93-94` → `AuthDomain.validateEmail` / `AuthDomain.validatePassword`, `core/auth/AuthDomain.kt:62,93`, `MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 8`) → `ClaimStep.Refused` → `AuthUiEvent.Error` → the error dialog.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/core/auth/AuthDomainTest.kt` — `validatePassword rejects too short`, `validateEmail` rejects an empty address; `LocalAccountClaimerTest` — `an invalid address is refused before the provider is asked`, `a wrong password changes nothing at all`; `AuthViewModelTest.a failed AttachAccount reports an error and keeps the local session`. **No class carries `AUTH-FAIL-03`.**
- **Must stay on-device**: the IME and `hideKeyboard` (the keyboard covers the submit button), the settings bottom-sheet path.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: a validation that is moved *after* the sign-in has already happened turns "your password is too short" into "your local data is gone" — the claimer's ordering is the guarantee, and only a claimer-level test sees the ordering.
- **Confidence**: high for the behaviour; note the flow's own comments are wrong about the disabled button and about a 6-character minimum (the app enforces 8).

### `auth/11-cold-install-login.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions**: on a cold, empty install the login screen is presented and usable (`auth_email_input`, `auth_continue_offline_button`), and **no spinner** is showing.
- **Production boundary**: the session restore inside `AuthRepository` (`core/auth/AuthRepository.kt`, the private `restoreSession` at line 345) behind the restore deadline, and the `Session.Loading → Session.SignedOut` transition; the failure presentation is `AuthRestoreFailureUiTest`'s subject.
- **Existing test file suitable for replacement**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/core/auth/AuthRepositoryRestoreBoundTest.kt` — `a restore that answers in time publishes the session it restored`, `a restore that ignores cancellation cannot hold the caller past the deadline`, `a restore that fails is answered the same way as one that misses the deadline`; and `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/auth/AuthRestoreFailureUiTest.kt` — `a failed restore reaches the screen as a message`, `a device with no session says nothing`. **No class carries `AUTH-FIRSTRUN-04`.**
- **Must stay on-device**: an install with no data at all, and the auth guard's very first frame.
- **Why a headless test catches a mattering regression**: the regression the flow names is a bricked app on a spinner — the guard never leaving `Session.Loading`. The deadline test already proves the repository cannot hold the caller past the budget; what is left is the guard's reaction, which is a ViewModel-level rule.
- **Confidence**: medium — the deadline is owned, the *first-frame* transition is what the flow adds, and it is cheap (18 s).

### Notes, search, projects

### `Maestro/flows/notes/01-edit-body.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the text typed in the note editor's body reaches the note; the preview renders that body read-only; the preview exposes the Edit action.
- **Production boundary**: the note editor's save path (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/notes/presentation/`) and the preview's rendering (`.../feature/notes/presentation/screen/NotePreviewScreen.kt`), which reads the note's body back from the repository.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/notes/NoteEditorFieldFlowTest.kt` — `the_body_typed_in_the_editor_reaches_the_note` — is the existing owner for the behaviour half; `NoteEditorDiscardGuardFlowTest.kt` owns the editor's back guard.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing into the quick-add and the body field, the **process kill + relaunch** against the on-device Room file (`helpers/relaunch.yaml`), and the title-addressed row id — the flow's own comment explains that a Material3 Card merges its descendants, so only the outer node is addressable in the Android accessibility tree.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: "the body was typed but never saved" is a silent data-loss defect; the desktop twin already asserts exactly that outcome in seconds, while the flow costs a full create → relaunch → reopen cycle.
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the split; **the flow fails in the three most recent sampled runs** (it passes in the two older ones), so which step is red is unverified, and no Room round-trip test for the note body was found — the "survives a restart" guarantee currently has no other owner.

### `Maestro/flows/notes/02-filter-chips.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the All chip shows every note; the Pinned chip shows only pinned notes; the Archived chip shows only archived notes; All restores the full list. The flow's own point is the *negative* one — a note that is neither pinned nor archived must disappear under Pinned and Archived.
- **Production boundary**: `NotesIntent.SetFilter(NoteFilter)` → `NotesListViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/notes/presentation/viewmodel/NotesListViewModel.kt:282,340`), which maps the filter to `repo.observeAll()` / `repo.watchPinned()` / `repo.watchArchived()` (lines 184-186) and re-subscribes to the source flow.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: **none exists** for the narrowing. `NotesListViewModelTest` (`shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/notes/NotesListViewModelTest.kt`) has `initial filter is All` and `setFilter updates filter state` — chip *state*, not the observed set — and `NotesFlowTest.the_filter_chips_are_offered_on_the_notes_list` (desktop) only asserts the chips are offered. The natural owner is a new case in `NotesListViewModelTest` that seeds a pinned, an archived and a plain note and asserts which ones each filter yields.
- **Must stay on-device**: typing two note titles through the IME and addressing the rows by their title-derived ids.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a filter chip that changes the label but not the query is the classic silent regression — the user taps "Pinned" and still sees everything; the mapping is three lines of a VM and needs no device.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/notes/03-internal-link-navigation.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a `task://<id>` anchor in a note's body is a link, not plain text; tapping it opens the referenced task; the task that opens is *that* task (the flow asserts the title, not merely "an editor opened").
- **Production boundary**: `NotePreviewScreen`'s `rememberAppUriHandler` → `onNavigateToTask` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/notes/presentation/screen/NotePreviewScreen.kt:455-470`), the shared grammar in `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/links/`, and `NotesNavigator.openTask` (`.../feature/notes/presentation/nav/NotesNavigator.kt`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/notes/presentation/nav/NotesNavigatorTest.kt` — `openTask exits to the task detail route carrying the id` — owns the *navigation*; the missing owner is the **tap** on the anchor, which would fit a desktop compose test on the note preview (the same tier as `NoteEditorFieldFlowTest`). `shared/src/commonTest/.../core/links/InternalLinksTest.kt` owns the grammar.
- **Must stay on-device**: the debug-seed fixture (a link carries an id a flow cannot know), the real tap on rendered anchor text, and the rendering of the anchor in the note body.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: an internal link that opens a browser or does nothing is the difference between a usable note and a dead one; the flow's own header spells out the three wrong outcomes it exists to exclude (browser, error, the note again).
- **Confidence**: medium — the navigation is owned, the tap is not.

### `Maestro/flows/notes/create-note.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the quick-add creates a note and navigates to the editor of the persisted note; saving puts the row in the list; the list offers the quick-add row itself.
- **Production boundary**: `NotesIntent.CreateNoteWithTitle` → `NotesListViewModel` (which persists and navigates to the repository's id) and `NotesNavigator`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `NotesListViewModelTest.createNoteWithTitle navigates to the id the repository persisted` (`shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/notes/NotesListViewModelTest.kt`) is the existing owner of the creation half; `NotesFlowTest.an_empty_notes_list_offers_to_create_the_first_note` (desktop) owns the list side.
- **Must stay on-device**: the Android app-menu path (`nav_menu_button` → `menu_sheet` → `menu_notes`, scrolled into view), the real IME "Done" action that submits the quick-add field, and the editor's autosave + explicit-save flush.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the flow's own header records that the quick-add creates the note **and** opens the editor — a regression that only creates the note leaves the user in the list, and vice versa; that navigation contract is exactly what the VM test owns.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/projects/01-open-detail.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the project card opens the project's detail; the detail shows that project's own task list (empty state first); the detail publishes its **own** title to the shell bar; the bar's back arrow pops the nested graph's inner stack (back to the list, not out of the app).
- **Production boundary**: `ProjectDetailViewModel` + `ProjectDetailBody` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/projects/presentation/`) and the title/back-action publication through `LocalScreenActions` / `ScreenActionsHolder` (`.../core/ui/components/`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/projects/presentation/screen/ProjectDetailTopBarShellPublishTest.kt` — `the project detail publishes its name and its back action to the shell bar` — owns the publication contract; `ProjectsFlowTest` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/flows/projects/`) owns `creating_a_project_puts_a_card_in_the_list` and `opening_a_project_reaches_its_detail_screen`.
- **Must stay on-device**: the Android menu's `menu_all_projects` row (it has to be scrolled *and* centred — the flow records that a tap on the last row of the sheet hits the gesture strip), and the shell's own `shell_bar_title` / `shell_back_button` / `top_bar_back_button` as resource ids.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the bar saying "Projects" while the screen shows a project is the defect `ProjectDetailTopBarShellPublishTest`'s header describes, and the shell's arrow leaving the whole graph instead of popping the inner stack is the second one; both are pure publication contracts.
- **Confidence**: high for the behaviour half.

### `Maestro/flows/projects/02-add-task.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a task created from the project's detail belongs to that project and appears in that project's list; the empty state is left.
- **Production boundary**: `ProjectDetailIntent.Domain.CreateTask` → `ProjectDetailViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/projects/presentation/viewmodel/ProjectDetailViewModel.kt`), which writes through the task repository and re-emits the project's task stream.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/projects/ProjectDetailViewModelTest.kt` — `CreateTask adds task to repository` — is the existing owner.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME "Done" action on `project_detail_quick_add`, and the row's tag `task_item_write_spec` in the project's list.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a task created from a project that lands outside it is a data-placement bug the user cannot see; the coordinator test asserts it without a device.
- **Confidence**: medium-high; the flow was red on 2026-11-21 and green on 2026-11-22, so the failing step is unverified.

### `Maestro/flows/projects/03-task-card-actions.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a touch row draws no trailing pin/delete buttons; the actions are offered on long press; running an action closes the menu; the delete row removes the task from the list.
- **Production boundary**: `TaskCard` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/tasks/presentation/components/TaskCard.kt:104` — `touchLayout: Boolean = isAndroidPlatform`) and the custom accessibility actions it declares; the delete is the project's soft delete.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/TaskCardTouchPresentationTest.kt` — `touch layout hides the trailing actions and offers them on long press` — already asserts the layout contract, including the announced custom actions.
- **Must stay on-device**: the long-press gesture and the `isAndroidPlatform` branch itself — the desktop test's own header says the Android wiring is covered end to end by the Maestro flows for project detail and search, i.e. by exactly these two flows.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: issue #2739 removed the trailing icons; the regression that matters is the actions becoming unreachable, and that is a layout contract the desktop tier can assert.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/projects/04-pick-emoji.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the picker offers the catalog; a shortcode query narrows it to the matching emoji; a query without a shortcode match says so; picking an emoji hands it back and the sheet closes; the pick reaches the project icon and survives a reopen.
- **Production boundary**: `IconPickerSheet` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/projects/presentation/components/IconPickerSheet.kt`) over the categories of `EmojiCatalog` (the Vanniktech provider, #2919) and the shortcode index; the write-through to the project is `updateProject` on the detail.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/projects/IconPickerRecentEmojiTest.kt` — `the search field narrows the catalog to a shortcode match`, `a query without a shortcode match says so`, `picking an emoji hands the emoji back` — owns the sheet's behaviour; `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/projects/ProjectIconRoomRoundTripTest.kt` owns the icon through Room.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing into `project_icon_search`, the sheet's own scroll on a phone, and the provider's **English** category label ("Faces" is `SmileysAndPeopleCategory`'s own name — no headless test asserts it, so this residue has no owner today).
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the sheet is unusable if the shortcode index does not answer, and that is a pure catalogue rule; the flow's own header is explicit that the pick is only real if it is persisted — which the Room round-trip already pins.
- **Confidence**: medium — the flow is red in every sampled run, so the failing step is unverified.

### `Maestro/flows/search/02-cyrillic.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a Cyrillic query matches a Cyrillic title; the matched title is drawn as Cyrillic rather than as mojibake; the row is addressable by the slug of the Cyrillic title (`task_item_покупки`).
- **Production boundary**: the tokenizer/normalizer behind the query (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/query/` — `QueryTokenizer`, `QueryNormalize`) and the FTS lookup; the addressing rule is `slug()` in `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/ui/TestTags.kt:1997`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/core/ui/SlugTest.kt` — `keeps non-latin letters addressable` — owns the **tag** half only. For the matching half there is **no owner**: grepping the tree for Cyrillic in `shared`, `desktopApp` and `androidApp` found only the `TestTags` comment ("not an invitation to type Cyrillic into flows") and three Russian-string resource tests. The natural home is a case in `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/search/SearchUseCaseFtsTest.kt` (real SQLite) plus a tokenizer case.
- **Must stay on-device**: injecting Cyrillic through `inputText` (the flow's header records that this was measured with two different keyboards) and the rendering of Cyrillic glyphs.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the flow's header explains that the word rule is a prefix rule over tokens, so a Latin "Pokupki" matches nothing — a transliteration/normalisation regression is invisible in a Latin-only test suite, which is exactly what the tree currently has.
- **Confidence**: medium — the flow is red in every sampled run and the headless owner does not exist yet.

### `Maestro/flows/search/03-clear.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the field's own clear button empties the query; the results disappear; the idle prompt returns.
- **Production boundary**: `SearchIntent.OnQueryChange("")` → `SearchViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/SearchViewModel.kt:116`) → the state decision `searchContent(query, results, isSearching)` (`.../feature/search/SearchContent.kt`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchPendingStateScenarioTest.kt` — `the_clear_button_empties_the_query_and_the_idle_prompt_returns` — drives the **same button** and asserts the same three outcomes; `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/SearchContentTest.kt` owns the idle-prompt decision and `SearchViewModelTest.blank query clears results and stops searching` owns the coordinator half.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing of the query and the clear button's resource id — both are exercised by the other search flows in this area, so nothing is lost if this flow is dropped.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the flow's own comment says the point is that the flow drives the *field's* button rather than the keyboard's erase action, so it fails if the button stops emptying the query — and the desktop twin drives that same button.
- **Confidence**: high.

### `Maestro/flows/search/04-filter-description.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: toggling the has-description filter on and applying it leaves only the tasks that carry a description; the free-text query keeps working while the filter is applied.
- **Production boundary**: `SimpleFilterBuilder.hasDescription` → `SimpleFilterMapper` → `Query.HasDescription` → `SearchQueryResolver` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/query/`), applied through `SearchIntent.OnApplyFilter` (`SearchViewModel.kt:127`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/query/SimpleFilterBuilderTest.kt` (`hasDescription sets hasDescription`) and `SimpleFilterMapperTest.kt` (`HasDescription maps to hasDescription true`, `filter with hasDescription maps to HasDescription`) own the mapping; `SearchViewModelTest.OnApplyFilter replaces the executed query` owns the coordinator. The **UI half** (toggle a switch in the sheet → Apply → the result set changes) has no owner: `SearchScreenSheetSmokeTest` only asserts that the sheet opens and its actions can be reached.
- **Must stay on-device**: the half-height sheet on a 360 dp phone (the switches and the actions live below the fold), the Tune icon's accessibility label (it has no testTag), and the real IME typing.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a filter that is displayed but never reaches the executed query is the silent failure the flow is designed to catch; the mapping and the coordinator are both headless-owned, only the sheet's UI path is missing.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/search/05-filter-pinned.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the pinned filter leaves only the pinned task; the contains mode finds a term in the middle of a word (the two titles share no prefix, so the flow must switch modes); the filter composes with the free-text query.
- **Production boundary**: `SimpleFilterBuilder.pinned` → `SimpleFilterMapper.IsPinned` → the executed query, and `SearchIntent.OnModeChange` (`SearchViewModel.kt:140`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `SimpleFilterBuilderTest.pinned sets pinned` and `SimpleFilterMapperTest.filter with pinned maps to IsPinned` own the mapping; `SearchContainsModeTest.the contains mode finds the term in the middle of a word` and `SearchViewModelTest.choosing the contains mode puts it in the state` own the mode.
- **Must stay on-device**: the sheet's switch and the Apply button below the fold on a phone, the `search_mode_contains` toggle, and the real IME typing.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a pinned filter that does not reach the executed query would leave the unpinned task visible — a wrong answer, not a missing one; the flow's own comment explains why the mode switch is needed (no common prefix).
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/search/06-no-results.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: an empty query shows the idle prompt; a query that matched nothing shows the no-results state **and not** the idle prompt.
- **Production boundary**: the pure decision `searchContent(query, results, isSearching)` in `shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchContent.kt`, and `SearchViewModel`'s state (`SearchUiState`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchContentTest.kt` — `an empty query shows the idle prompt`, `a query that matched nothing shows the no-results state`, `a query that matched nothing does not show the idle prompt` — owns the decision; `SearchViewModelTest.an empty query leaves the screen on the idle prompt` / `a query that matches nothing puts the screen on the no-results state` own the coordinator. What is **not** owned: the tags `search_empty_placeholder` / `search_empty_results` reaching the screen — `SearchContentTest`'s own header says the composable that draws it has no Compose harness in that module, and I found no headless assertion of either tag.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing of the non-matching query and the two tags as ids.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the two states exist precisely to be distinguishable; showing the idle prompt after a failed query is the defect, and it is a one-line decision.
- **Confidence**: medium-high. Note that `negative/05-empty-search.yaml` (area A) claims the same behaviour — two flows, one guarantee; only one of them should keep the device slot.

### `Maestro/flows/search/07-saved-search.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: saving the current query under a name persists it; the saved-search row offers the saved searches whose name or stored query matches the current query; tapping a saved search restores its query and runs it; a saved search loaded and then edited dispatches the query the field holds.
- **Production boundary**: `SearchIntent.OnSaveCurrentSearch` / `OnLoadSavedSearch` (`SearchViewModel.kt:129,131`) → the saved-search repository (Room) → `SavedSearchesRow`; the row's filtering is the pure `savedSearchSuggestions(ALL, query)` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/SavedSearchSuggestions.kt`).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `SearchViewModelTest` — `OnSaveCurrentSearch persists the current query and sets activeSavedSearchId`, `OnLoadSavedSearch restores the query string and triggers search`, `a saved search carries the query that runs, not the text that no longer says it`, `editing a loaded saved search dispatches the query the field holds` — plus `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/SavedSearchSuggestionsTest.kt` (`a blank query offers every saved search`, `the query filters by saved-search name, case-insensitively`) and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/search/SearchPresentationUiTest.kt` for the dialog.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing of the name (the field is prefilled with the query, so the flow must `eraseText` first), the dialog's tags, the chip's slug id, and the read-back **after leaving and re-entering the screen**.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: "saved but not loaded" and "loaded but the wrong query runs" were the two real defects; both are coordinator contracts the VM test owns, and the desktop row test owns the chip.
- **Confidence**: medium — the flow is red in every sampled run and the read-back path is the part that only the device exercises.

### `Maestro/flows/search/08-word-prefix.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a term matches the **beginning** of a word ("arch" finds "Architecture of the house") and does not match its middle ("port" does not find "Airport run").
- **Production boundary**: the word-prefix rule (F2a in the search grammar) in `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/query/` — the tokenizer and the FTS query — executed by `SearchQueryResolver`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/SearchContainsModeTest.kt` — `the word mode finds the word that begins with the term` and `the word mode does not find a term in the middle of a word` — asserts **both** answers of the flow against the same fixture; `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/search/SearchUseCaseFtsTest.kt` — `a word that merely contains the term is not found` — asserts the negative answer against a real SQLite index.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing and the on-device Room build's FTS index — the latter is exercised by the other search flows in this area, so dropping this flow loses no unique guarantee.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the word rule is the search grammar's core contract, and the flow's own header states that the second answer must be about a record that *was* there — the headless fixture seeds both records too.
- **Confidence**: medium-high (the flow was red on 2026-11-21 and green on 2026-11-22, so the failure is unexplained).

### `Maestro/flows/search/09-contains-mode.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the same query answers differently in the two modes — "port" does not find "Airport run" in word mode and does in contains mode.
- **Production boundary**: `SearchMode` and `SearchIntent.OnModeChange` (`SearchViewModel.kt:140`) plus the contains-mode matcher in `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/query/`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `SearchContainsModeTest` — `the word mode does not find a term in the middle of a word` and `the contains mode finds the term in the middle of a word` — asserts the identical pair; `SearchViewModelTest.the contains mode finds what the word mode does not` and `choosing the contains mode puts it in the state` own the coordinator; `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/search/SearchModeSwitchUiTest.kt` owns the switch.
- **Must stay on-device**: the `search_mode_contains` tag as a resource id and the real IME typing — shared with the other search flows.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the contains mode is the documented escape hatch of the word rule (F6); a mode switch that changes the label but not the executed query is the regression, and it is asserted headlessly.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/search/10-task-card-actions.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a touch result row draws no trailing pin button; the pin action is reachable on long press; running it closes the menu; the row keeps the state it was found with, because a search result is a snapshot of a query (#2810).
- **Production boundary**: `TaskCard`'s touch layout (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/tasks/presentation/components/TaskCard.kt:104`) and `SearchIntent.OnTogglePin` (`SearchViewModel.kt:137`) → the task repository's `togglePinned`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/tasks/TaskCardTouchPresentationTest.kt` — `touch layout hides the trailing actions and offers them on long press` — owns the layout; `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../feature/search/SearchSuggestionInteractionUiTest.kt` — `pinning a result runs the action and keeps the suggestions` — owns the action's effect; `SearchViewModelTest.OnTogglePin delegates to taskRepository togglePinned without error` owns the coordinator.
- **Must stay on-device**: the long-press gesture and the `isAndroidPlatform` branch — the desktop test's header names this Maestro flow as the owner of that Android wiring.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: issue #2739 removed the trailing pin icon; if the long-press route stopped working the action would be unreachable, and the desktop test can assert the *offered* actions even though only a device can assert the gesture.
- **Confidence**: medium-high.

### `Maestro/flows/search/11-filter-sheet-reachability.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: after the sheet's body is scrolled to the end, the Apply and Cancel actions and the last sort chip are in the tree and can be pressed; Cancel closes the sheet.
- **Production boundary**: `SimpleFilterSheet` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/SimpleFilterSheet.kt`) and the sheet's scrolling body; the actions carry `search_filter_apply_button` / `search_filter_cancel_button`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchScreenSheetSmokeTest.kt` — `filter sheet actions are reachable in a short window` — already measures the same 360 dp window, and `filter sheet opens from the top bar and its actions can be reached` covers the opening.
- **Must stay on-device**: the **resource ids** inside a modal bottom sheet (`mapTestTagsAsResourceIds`), the half-height sheet on a phone, the system navigation strip, and the Tune icon's accessibility label (no testTag). This is the guarantee the flow's own header explains — "the sheet that put its actions in a header at the top lost them from the accessibility tree as soon as the body was scrolled" — and no faster tier can observe the Android accessibility tree.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the actions becoming untappable is a silent loss of function; the desktop twin already covers the semantics half at the same window size.
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the split; the resource-id half needs an explicit owner (a smaller device flow, or a device instrumented test).

### `Maestro/flows/search/12-no-results-recovery.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a no-match **without** filters offers only the clear-query action; clearing the query empties the field and brings the idle prompt back; a no-match **because of** filters offers the clear-filters action; taking the filters away brings the hidden result back, and the free text and the mode survive it.
- **Production boundary**: `SearchScreen.kt:289` — `onClearFilters` → `SearchIntent.OnApplyFilter(filter.withoutStructuredConstraints())` — so the filter that goes through the intent is the one the search runs; plus `SearchIntent.OnApplyFilter` and the query execution in `SearchViewModel`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchResultContextUiTest.kt` — `a no-match without filters offers only the clear-query action`, `a no-match with structured filters offers the clear-filters action`, `the clear-filters action sends the filter without its structured constraints`, `the clear-query action clears the field` — plus `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/presentation/viewmodel/SearchViewModelTest.kt` — `clearing the filters brings back the results the constraints had hidden`, `clearing the structured constraints changes the executed query and keeps the free text`, `clearing the filters does not clear the query text`.
- **Must stay on-device**: the sheet's status chip by its label, the real IME typing, and the two clear actions as resource ids.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the flow's own header says the second half "is the one that can lie" — a "clear filters" action that only cleared the displayed filter would leave the results empty; the desktop twin asserts exactly the *filter that goes through the intent*, and the VM test asserts that the results come back.
- **Confidence**: medium — every guarantee is owned headlessly, but the flow is red in **every** sampled run, so the failing step must be root-caused before the flow is dropped (a red device flow whose behaviour is green headlessly usually means the device path, not the contract).

### `Maestro/flows/search/13-suggestion-dismissal.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: an outside tap dismisses the suggestion row and leaves the query alone; the row is collapsed, not removed (the current query can still be saved); focusing the field again offers the suggestions again; tapping a result while the row is on screen dismisses the row **and** opens the task.
- **Production boundary**: the suggestion state and the dismissal handlers in `SearchScreen` / `SearchViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/`), and the pure row filter `savedSearchSuggestions(ALL, query)`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchSuggestionInteractionUiTest.kt` — `outside tap dismisses suggestions without changing the query`, `outside tap dismisses suggestions with empty query`, `focusing the input restores dismissed suggestions`, `every kind of result navigates and dismisses the suggestions`, `clear restores suggestions after dismissal`, `save opens the dialog after the suggestions are dismissed` — asserts each of those outcomes case by case; `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/SavedSearchSuggestionsTest.kt` owns the row's filtering.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME typing of the name and the query, the dialog's tags, the chip's slug id, and the `LazyRow` composition of the chip row on a phone.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the flow's own header names the defect — an outside tap that swallowed the result's own click would leave the person on a screen that looks like it ignored them; the desktop twin asserts that "every kind of result navigates and dismisses the suggestions".
- **Confidence**: medium — the flow is red in every sampled run, and the one residue I could not pin to a named test is "the Save chip stays visible after the dismissal" (the desktop class covers the dialog opening after a dismissal, not the chip's continued visibility).

### Menu, navigation, system

### `menu/03-open-project.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** after seeding one project, the menu's Projects section is unfolded, the row addressed by the project's **id** (`menu_project_node_roadmap`) is visible and is tapped, and the assertion is that the **project's own screen opens** (`project_detail_quick_add` becomes visible). Nothing about the sheet, the scroll or the id survives into the behaviour.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/projects/presentation/nav/ProjectsOpener.kt` — `ProjectsOpenRequest.Project`, `requestOpen(projectId)`, `consume()` — consumed by `ProjectsNavigator.openDetail` (`.../nav/ProjectsNavigator.kt`). The Android sheet's side of the same boundary is `AndroidShellNav3.kt`'s `onOpenProject = { projectsOpener.requestOpen(projectId); navigator.open(ProjectsGraph()) }`; the desktop drawer reaches the same common code. The row the flow taps is `ProjectsMenuSection` → `ProjectTreeSection` → `menuProjectNode(id)`.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/ProjectsMenuOpenScenarioTest.kt` — already the carrier of scenario **MENU-02**, the same id the flow claims. No new class is needed; the flow's behaviour is already asserted there.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the row must be addressable as an **Android resource id** (`menu_project_node_roadmap`) inside the sheet's own window, and the section starts folded with the row below the fold (`scrollUntilVisible`). If the id-addressability is worth keeping, it belongs in the instrumented `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt` (which already asserts `requiredMenuTags()` by `By.res`), not in a Maestro flow.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if `ProjectsOpener.requestOpen` stopped carrying the id (or `ProjectsNavigator.openDetail` stopped pushing the detail), the desktop carrier fails immediately; on-device the same regression would surface as a 5 s Maestro timeout with no clue about which of the two halves broke.
- **Confidence:** high.

### `menu/04-all-projects.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** the menu's Projects section carries an item (`menu_all_projects`) that opens the **full project list**, and the list contains the seeded project (`project_card_roadmap`). The behaviour is "the tree is a way into the projects, not the only way".
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../shell/AppMenu.kt` — `sheetDestinations` / `allDestinations` and `menuItemId(destination)`; the Projects section's "all projects" item in `shell/ProjectsMenuSection.kt` (`MENU_ALL_PROJECTS`), which on selection goes through the same `ProjectsOpener.requestList()` / `ProjectsOpenRequest.List` path the Android shell uses (`AndroidShellNav3.kt`, `onSelect = { projectsOpener.requestList() }`).
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/ProjectsMenuAllProjectsScenarioTest.kt` — already the carrier of scenario **MENU-06**, the same id the flow claims.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** that the item is reachable **as a resource id** after a downward scroll in the sheet's own window. That property is already asserted device-side by `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt` (`MENU_ALL_PROJECTS` is in `requiredMenuTags()`), so the flow does not need to keep it.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if the "all projects" item were dropped from the menu model or pointed at the wrong destination, the desktop carrier names the broken link; the Maestro flow would only report "project_card_roadmap never appeared".
- **Confidence:** high.

### `menu/06-tree-live.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** the menu's Projects section is **live** — a project created elsewhere (`menu_project_node_latecomer`) and a project renamed elsewhere (the same row `menu_project_node_roadmap`, now reading "Renamed") both appear in the section after it is read again.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** the section is drawn from the profile's projects, not from a snapshot: `shell/ProjectsMenuSection.kt` → `ProjectTreeSection` over the projects state, fed by the projects repository/use case behind `feature/projects/presentation/…`; the common-code boundary is the projects flow that both the sheet and the drawer observe. The flow's own header names the split: "the liveness itself … is what the Desktop drawer's test covers, where the drawer and the app share one window".
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/ProjectsMenuLiveScenarioTest.kt` — the carrier of scenario **MENU-03**, which asserts the section updating **while the drawer is open** (a project created elsewhere appears without a reopen). It covers the **creation** half only: the flow's rename half (the row reading "Renamed") is asserted by no tier, so a follow-up would have to add it to this class first than the flow (the flow must reopen the sheet, because a change on another surface closes it).
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the reopen-the-sheet path — that a change made on another surface closes the modal sheet and the section is current on the **next** opening. If that is worth keeping, it is a statement about the Android modal sheet, and it is best kept as a small instrumented assertion or a deliberately thin Maestro flow — not as the carrier of MENU-03's liveness claim.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if the section were ever fed from a cached snapshot instead of the projects flow, the desktop carrier (in-window) fails on the very next write, while the Maestro flow's extra reopen could mask it by re-reading a fresh snapshot.
- **Confidence:** medium — the creation half is owned headlessly and more strongly, but the rename half has no owner, and the sheet's dismiss-on-change semantics are a real Android property that a replacement must consciously drop or hand to the device tier.

### `menu/10-large-projects.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** with 120 projects the menu's destinations stay on screen without scrolling through the projects (`menu_settings` visible), the way into the full list stays reachable (`menu_all_projects`), the Projects section starts **folded** (`menu_projects_expand` present, `menu_projects_collapse` absent) and no project row is rendered while folded (`menu_project_node_bulk_001` not visible); unfolding the section reveals the project row.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../shell/ProjectsMenuSection.kt` — the section's `state.sectionExpanded` gate and the toggle whose tag is `MENU_PROJECTS_EXPAND` while folded / `MENU_PROJECTS_COLLAPSE` while unfolded, plus `feature/projects/presentation/components/ProjectTreeSection.kt` (`menuProjectNode(id)`) which is drawn only when expanded.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/ProjectsMenuCollapsedScenarioTest.kt` — the carrier of scenario **MENU-07**, which seeds 120 projects and asserts the fold, the reachability of `menu_all_projects`, the absence of `menu_project_node("bulk-001")` while folded, its presence after expanding, and the "Collapse projects" description.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the **two different ids** of the toggle (`menu_projects_expand` vs `menu_projects_collapse`) and the fact that both are Android resource ids inside the sheet's own window; also the sheet's scroll behaviour with 120 rows. The id pair is a good candidate for the instrumented `MenuSheetBoundsTest` (it already asserts the expand/collapse pair by `By.res`).
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if the section stopped folding — the projects would be drawn inline and push the destinations off-screen — the desktop carrier fails on the fold assertion, whereas on-device the failure appears as "menu_settings never became visible" with the 120-row seed as a confound.
- **Confidence:** high.

### `nav/02-menu-parity.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** the Calendar is a bar tab (`nav_tab_calendar` visible) and **not** a menu entry; the menu's shared content carries the way into the full list (`menu_all_projects`) and the seeded project (`menu_project_node_roadmap`).
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** the parity itself is a **model** question: `shared/src/commonMain/.../shell/AppMenu.kt` (`destinations`, `sheetDestinations`, `allDestinations`) against `feature/nav/AppDestination.kt` (`DestinationKind.tabs` = Inbox, Today, Upcoming, Calendar; `DestinationKind.menuEntries` = Statistics, Notes, AiChat, Proposals, Search, Archive, Pomodoro, ProfileSwitcher, Settings, AiUsage). Both surfaces — the Android sheet (`MenuBottomSheet.kt:117-123`) and the desktop drawer (`DesktopShellNav3.kt`) — read that one model.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/MenuParityFlowTest.kt` — the carrier of scenario **NAV-02**, which asserts `DestinationKind.menuEntries.toSet() == AppMenu.allDestinations`, that Calendar is not in the menu, that Pomodoro is, and "the drawer offers the entries of the shared menu model". That is a strictly stronger, drift-proof version of the flow's three spot-checks.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the tab id `nav_tab_calendar` is an Android resource id (`AndroidShellNav3.kt:150`), and the sheet's rows must be resource ids inside their own window — the latter is already covered by the instrumented `A/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt`.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** parity is exactly the claim that breaks when someone adds a destination to one list and forgets the other; the model-level assertion fails on the commit that introduces the divergence, while the Maestro flow would keep passing as long as its three spot-checked entries remain.
- **Confidence:** high.

### `nav/bottom-nav-tabs.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** on Inbox, Today and Upcoming the shell FAB (`tasks_fab`) is visible; on Calendar and on Pomodoro (reached through the menu sheet) it is not. The flow's own header states the rule's source: "The FAB expectations encode the real contract from `FabActionResolver`".
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../shell/FabActionResolver.kt` (with `shellFabTarget`) — the classifier that turns a destination into a FAB action or a named no-action exception; the Android side is `AndroidShellNav3.kt:189` (`TASKS_FAB`).
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** two existing owners, both sufficient — `shared/src/commonTest/.../shell/FabActionResolverTest.kt` (`everyAgendaTabInTheBottomBar_returnsATaskCreationAction`, `everyNonAgendaTab_isANamedNoActionException`, `everyDestination_resolvesToTheClassifiedAction`) for the rule, and `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/NavigationSmokeTest.kt` (`fab_visibility_matches_tab_contract` — FAB present on Inbox/Upcoming, absent on Calendar and Pomodoro) for the Android consequence, by resource id.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the **tab resource ids** (`nav_tab_inbox`, `nav_tab_today`, `nav_tab_upcoming`, `nav_tab_calendar`) and the FAB's own id `tasks_fab` — all applied only in `AndroidShellNav3.kt`. The device coverage of the FAB contract already exists in `NavigationSmokeTest`; the tab ids are covered by `nav/01-no-calendar-tab.yaml` (which stays) and by `NavigationSmokeTest.app_launches_and_all_tabs_are_reachable`.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if a new destination were added to `DestinationKind.tabs` without a FAB decision, `FabActionResolverTest.everyDestination_resolvesToTheClassifiedAction` fails on the host in seconds; on-device the same regression shows up as a FAB hovering over a screen that has no task list.
- **Confidence:** high — but note the flow is a **smoke** flow, so removing it changes PR-path cost; the replacement (instrumented `NavigationSmokeTest` + host `FabActionResolverTest`) must be in place before the flow is dropped.

### `system/menu-sheet.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** the menu sheet opens and exposes its documented items — `menu_settings`, `menu_profiles` (Account section), `menu_quick_search` (Search section), and `menu_statistics`, `menu_notes`, `menu_aichat`, `menu_archive` (Destinations section). Visibility only; the flow says so: "this does not navigate into each destination".
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../shell/AppMenu.kt` — `account` (`MENU_SETTINGS` "Profile & sync", `MENU_PROFILES`), `search` (`MENU_QUICK_SEARCH`), `destinations` (= `DestinationKind.menuEntries` mapped), `sheetDestinations` (destinations minus the account/search ones); the sheet composes the three sections in `shell/MenuBottomSheet.kt:117-123` and tags each item with `Modifier.mapTestTagsAsResourceIds().testTag(item.id)` (lines 136-140).
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt` — its `requiredMenuTags()` is **derived from `AppMenu`** (`AppMenu.account` + `AppMenu.search` + `AppMenu.sheetDestinations` ids, plus `MENU_PROJECTS_HEADER`, `MENU_ALL_PROJECTS`, the expand/collapse pair, plus `MENU_ATTACH_ACCOUNT` if rendered) and every one is asserted **by `By.res`**, i.e. as a resource id inside the sheet's own window — the flow's hand-written subset is a strict subset of this. The model half is owned by `shared/src/commonTest/.../shell/AppMenuTest.kt`.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the sheet renders in **its own window**, so the entries' reachability as **resource ids** is Android-only by construction (`mapTestTagsAsResourceIds()` has a no-op jvm actual in `shared/src/jvmMain/.../core/ui/TestTagResourceId.jvm.kt`), and the sheet's bounds on compact and expanded widths are device geometry. Both are already in `MenuSheetBoundsTest` — which is the replacement, not a reason to keep the flow.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** the flow's list is hand-written and can silently drift from the model (a renamed id, a moved section, a dropped entry). The derived list in `MenuSheetBoundsTest` cannot drift: adding a destination to `AppMenu` immediately makes the device test demand it in the sheet.
- **Confidence:** high.

### `menu/05-tree-contents.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** a childless project is listed in the menu's Projects section (`menu_project_node_childless` visible) and, by construction, carries no expander — the flow says it outright: "Nothing here says it has no expander: the expander is a child of the row, and a project without children has none."
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/projects/presentation/components/ProjectTreeSection.kt` — a node renders an expander only when it has children, and the rows are `menuProjectNode(id)` / `menuProjectExpander(id)`; the section above it is `shell/ProjectsMenuSection.kt`.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/ProjectsMenuExpanderScenarioTest.kt` — the carrier of scenario **MENU-05**, which asserts **both** halves of the rule: a childless project offers no expander, and a closed project is absent from the section. The flow asserts only the first half; the second (closed project absent) is asserted nowhere on-device.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the row must be reachable as an Android **resource id** after the section is unfolded and scrolled to, inside the sheet's own window.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if the tree started rendering expander buttons on childless rows (a classic "invites an expansion that cannot happen" defect), the desktop carrier fails on the expander assertion; the Maestro flow as written would not notice at all, because it never asserts the absence of `menu_project_expander_childless`.
- **Confidence:** high for the behaviour split; the device residue is small enough that the instrumented `MenuSheetBoundsTest` may be a better home than a Maestro flow.

### `menu/07-ai-chat-entry.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** the menu's AI-chat entry (`menu_aichat`) is visible in the sheet, is tapped, and the **AI chat screen opens** (`chat_input` visible).
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../shell/AppMenu.kt` — the AI chat is a `DestinationKind.menuEntries` entry, so the sheet and the drawer select the same `AppDestination`; `feature/nav/AppDestination.kt` (`AiChat`) and `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/ui/components/ChatInputBar.kt:68` (`CHAT_INPUT`) are the shared surface the flow lands on.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/ai/AiChatNavigationFlowTest.kt` — "the menu opens the AI chat, and the shell's arrow leaves it again" (asserts `CHAT_INPUT`), i.e. the behaviour plus the exit.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** `menu_aichat` as an **Android resource id** in the sheet's own window, after a downward scroll. `AppMenu`'s model already guarantees the entry exists; the device-specific claim is only the id's reachability, and `A/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt` proves the whole entry set that way.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if the AI-chat entry were removed from `AppMenu.destinations` or pointed at the wrong destination, the desktop flow test fails on the missing `CHAT_INPUT`; on-device the same regression is a 5 s timeout on `chat_input` with no indication whether the entry or the screen broke.
- **Confidence:** medium-high — the behaviour is owned headlessly, but the flow's only unique asset is one id, and the flow failed on 2026-11-21.

### `menu/08-proposals-entry.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** the menu's proposals entry (`menu_proposals`) is visible, is tapped, and the **proposals screen opens showing its empty state** (`proposals_empty` visible).
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `AppMenu.destinations` (a `DestinationKind.menuEntries` entry → `AppDestination.Proposals`) and `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/proposals/presentation/ProposalsScreen.kt:86`, which applies `PROPOSALS_EMPTY` to the empty state. Both are common code — the route and the empty state are portable; only the menu row's id and the sheet's window are Android's.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** none exists today — "none found" was the outcome of the search for a class referencing `menu_proposals` or `proposals_empty`. The natural home is a new desktop class beside `D/feature/flows/ai/AiChatNavigationFlowTest.kt` (the same "a menu entry opens its screen" shape), asserting the entry's destination resolves to `Proposals` and that the screen renders its empty state; the desktop drawer renders the same `AppMenu.destinations`.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** `menu_proposals` as an Android resource id in the sheet, and the empty state as a resource id on the proposals screen.
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** this is a whole menu destination with **no Kotlin carrier of any kind** — a rename of the empty-state tag, or the entry being dropped from the menu, would today be caught only by this flow, and the failure mode of dropping it entirely is that a destination quietly disappears from the menu with no failing test anywhere.
- **Confidence:** medium — the split is clear (the rule is portable, the id is not), but the headless half has to be **written**; there is no existing file to point at, and a flow that is the sole guard on a destination should not be dropped before that exists.

### `system/search-finds-task.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** typing a known task title into the search input surfaces that task in the results (`task_item_uniquesearchabletask2026` visible); erasing the text removes it again (the row is no longer visible).
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/search/presentation/viewmodel/SearchViewModel.kt` (query → results state) over `feature/search/…/SearchUseCase` (the FTS query) and the shared `SearchContent`/`SearchScreen` rendering; the menu path is `AppMenu.search` → `MENU_QUICK_SEARCH` → `AppDestination.Search`.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/search/SearchPendingStateScenarioTest.kt` — the desktop carrier of **SEARCH-01**, the same id the flow claims, plus the clear-button, focus and saved-search cases; the query rule itself is in `shared/src/jvmTest/.../feature/search/SearchUseCaseFtsTest.kt` and the view model in `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/presentation/viewmodel/SearchViewModelTest.kt`.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the **real IME** (`inputText` and `eraseText` against the platform keyboard) and the menu→quick-search entry path inside the sheet's window (the `menu_quick_search` id as a resource id).
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if the FTS query stopped matching a title or the clear action stopped clearing, the desktop carrier and `SearchUseCaseFtsTest` fail immediately; the Maestro version of the same failure is a 55 s flow whose only symptom is a timeout on a row id.
- **Confidence:** high for the split — but note the flow is a **smoke** flow (it failed on the smoke run of 2026-11-23 with the harness symptom `Assertion is false: id: nav_tab_today is visible`, i.e. the app never launched), so the IME residue must be kept somewhere before the flow is dropped.

### `system/seed-unknown-key.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only Maestro assertions:** a seed query containing an unknown key (`blockeddBy=MissingBlocker`, a deliberate misspelling of `blockedBy`) still seeds the keys it knows — the row `task_item_buy_milk` is visible on the Inbox tab.
- **Production coordinator/intent/action boundary:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/seed/SeedLinkKeys.kt` — `unknown(keys)` (line 105) classifies the query's keys; the Android consumer is `androidApp/src/debug/.../debug/DebugSeedActivity.kt:315-320`, which logs `Unknown seed query key '$key'; it was ignored` per unknown key and then applies the known ones.
- **Exact existing test file suitable for the replacement:** `shared/src/commonTest/.../core/seed/SeedLinkKeysTest.kt` — owns the classification ("the historical typo is unknown", control keys known, delete keys, every key has a kind) in common code, where the rule actually lives.
- **Platform assertions that must stay on-device:** the **logcat report** — the only evidence that the unknown key was *noticed* rather than silently dropped — and the delivery of the seed as a debug deep link into the Android debug build (`todo-debug://seed?…`, `DebugSeedActivity`, `singleTask`).
- **Why a headless test would catch a regression that matters:** if `SeedLinkKeys.unknown` stopped excluding a key (or started excluding a good one), `SeedLinkKeysTest` fails on the host; on-device the same defect appears as "the row never appeared", with the logcat line as the only clue, and only in a debug build.
- **Confidence:** high for the split; the logcat assertion would have to be an instrumented/log-based test rather than a JVM test, since the message is produced by an Android-only debug class.

---

### Smoke, settings, sync, profile, pomodoro

Each candidate names: the behaviour-only assertions of the flow, the production boundary they cross, the exact existing file that should own them, the assertions that must stay on the device, why a headless test catches a regression that matters, and a confidence note. "Existing file" means a file that exists in the tree today — where the guarantee has no owner, the closest existing owner is named and the gap is stated.

### `Maestro/flows/smoke/02-menu-settings.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: selecting the menu's Settings entry opens the Settings surface; the settings surface offers its tab trigger.
- **Production boundary**: the app-menu destination (`AppDestination`/`DestinationKind.menuEntries`) → the Settings route → `SettingsScreen` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/settings/SettingsScreen.kt`), whose narrow layout is the `settings_tab_dropdown` trigger.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/menu/MenuParityFlowTest.kt` (NAV-02 — every menu entry is offered) already owns "the menu can reach Settings"; the device sheet's own bounds are owned by `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt`.
- **Must stay on-device**: the sheet row's reachability by scrolling, and the **system back** returning to `nav_tab_today`.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a menu entry that no longer routes to Settings is a dead end; that is already owned, so the flow's own value is the device residue.
- **Confidence**: medium — the split is about the residue, not about a missing behaviour owner.

### `Maestro/flows/smoke/03-account-tab.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a signed-in session renders the sign-out action (`TestTags.AUTH_SIGN_OUT_BUTTON`) in the Account tab.
- **Production boundary**: `AccountSettingsScreen` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/profile/presentation/AccountSettingsScreen.kt`) → the session model (`Session.SignedIn` vs `Session.Anonymous`) → the card list.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/profile/presentation/AccountSettingsScreenTest.kt` — `a signed in session names the account` (asserts `AUTH_SIGN_OUT_BUTTON`).
- **Must stay on-device**: reaching a **trailing** tab through the narrow sheet (Account is the last of 13 rows) and the scroll that brings the sign-out card above the fold.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a build that renders the attach affordance for a signed-in session (the mistake that made `auth/07-account-attach.yaml` wait forever) would pass this flow but break the user; the desktop test asserts the branch on the session model.
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the split; the narrow-sheet half has no owner at any tier (see Uncertainties).

### `Maestro/flows/smoke/03-health-drilldown.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: tapping the Blocked bucket lands on the agenda's Blocked section; tapping the Stagnant bucket lands on the "Not touched in a while" section; **tapping the Overdue bucket lands on the agenda's Overdue section** — the last one has no owner today.
- **Production boundary**: `StatisticsScreen`'s `onBucketClick` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/statistics/StatisticsScreen.kt:179-199`) → `navCallbacks?.navigate(AppDestination.AgendaGraph(AgendaStartRoute.Overdue | Blocked | Stagnant))` → the agenda's merged tree, whose sections carry `TestTags.agendaSection(name)`.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/agenda/AgendaSectionTagReachabilityFlowTest.kt` — `the_blocked_health_drilldown_exposes_the_blocked_section_in_the_merged_tree` and `the_stagnant_health_drilldown_exposes_the_stagnant_section_in_the_merged_tree` already own two of the three. The **overdue** drill-down has no owner: `StatisticsHealthTabFlowTest.the_health_tab_is_reachable_and_carries_its_own_id` asserts `HEALTH_BUCKET_OVERDUE` is *displayed*, and `AgendaOverdueSectionUiTest` asserts the Overdue *section*, but no test clicks the overdue bucket.
- **Must stay on-device**: the **system back** from the agenda to Statistics (the agenda has no in-app back button) and the seeded fixture's arrival as a section.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a bucket that navigates to the wrong agenda preset shows the user an empty list — the click is the whole point of the bucket, and the overdue branch is a separate `when` arm from the two that are covered.
