- **Confidence**: medium-high for blocked + stagnant; **medium** for overdue (new case, same file, same pattern as the two existing ones).

### `Maestro/flows/smoke/06-session-anonymous.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: an **anonymous** session renders the attach affordance (`AUTH_ATTACH_ACCOUNT_BUTTON`) and does **not** render sign-out (`AUTH_SIGN_OUT_BUTTON`).
- **Production boundary**: `AuthRepository.startLocalSession()` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../core/auth/AuthRepository.kt:102`, the same call the "Continue offline" button makes via `AuthViewModel`/`LocalAccountClaimer`) → `Session.Anonymous` → `AccountSettingsScreen`'s branch on the session.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/profile/presentation/AccountSettingsScreenTest.kt` — `an anonymous session says so and names no account` (asserts `AUTH_ATTACH_ACCOUNT_BUTTON` present, `AUTH_SIGN_OUT_BUTTON` absent).
- **Must stay on-device**: the `session=anonymous` deep link itself — it is a **debug-only** link (`androidApp/src/debug/…/DebugSeedActivity.kt`), absent from a release APK — and the narrow sheet's Account tab.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: the attach affordance must exist exactly for a local session; the flow exists because the E2E build's stub session is `SignedIn` from the first frame, and the desktop test covers the branch the stub cannot reach.
- **Confidence**: high for the session→surface half; the deep link stays by construction.

### `Maestro/flows/settings/01-theme-toggle.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the three-way theme selector and the accent swatch are offered; selecting Dark mode **changes the theme** (the observable claim the Maestro flow cannot make) and the rest of the tab survives.
- **Production boundary**: `SettingsIntent.Appearance.UpdateThemeMode` → `AppearanceSettingsStore` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../core/settings/SettingsBundle.kt`, `core/appearance/AppearanceSettingsStore.kt`) → `SingularityTheme`; the live preview in `InterfaceSettingsScreen` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/settings/screens/`).
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/settings/InterfaceSettingsScreenPreviewTest.kt` — `selecting a theme mode repaints the preview` (a pixel comparison of the preview before/after the selection) — owns the behaviour the flow cannot assert.
- **Must stay on-device**: the real system-wide theme application and the segmented selector's rendering inside the narrow sheet. (Neither is asserted by the flow either — the flow asserts survival only.)
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: "the user's choice is ignored" is the regression the spec SETTINGS-01 names, and the flow would pass against it; the desktop test fails.
- **Confidence**: high — the desktop test is a strict superset of the flow's guarantee.

### `Maestro/flows/settings/02-notifications.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the Notifications tab renders the enabled switch and the sound switch; and, behind them, the rule "with the permission denied the app must not post" and "the toggle writes the enabled state".
- **Production boundary**: `NotificationsSettingsContributor` / `NotificationsSettingsStore` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../core/notifications/`) → `SettingsViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/settings/SettingsViewModel.kt`) → `NotificationSupport` → `Notifier`.
- **Existing file**: `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/core/notifications/NotificationSupportTest.kt` (the permission matrix), `shared/src/androidHostTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/core/notifications/AndroidNotifierTest.kt` (the Android port) and `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/settings/SettingsViewModelTest.kt` — `Notifications UpdateEnabled updates state`.
- **Must stay on-device**: nothing platform-specific is left — but the *presence* of the two switches has **no owner in any tier**; the natural home would be a desktop compose case opening the Notifications tab (the same `clickSettingsTab("Notifications")` pattern as `SyncSettings01ScenarioTest`), which does not exist.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a Notifications tab that renders nothing is exactly the failure SETTINGS-02 exists for, and it is invisible to the current owners, which test the *rules* rather than the *surface*.
- **Confidence**: medium — the behaviour owners are certain, the surface owner is a proposed new case.

### `Maestro/flows/settings/03-cycle-tabs.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: each of the 13 Settings tabs opens and renders without crashing; every tab is addressable exactly once in the settings menu; every tab belongs to exactly one group.
- **Production boundary**: `SettingsTab` / `SETTINGS_GROUPS` / `groupedLabel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/settings/SettingsNavigation.kt`) → `SettingsMenu.kt`'s `SETTINGS_MENU_ROWS` → `SettingsViewModel`'s per-section contributors → `SettingsScreen`'s narrow `settings_tab_dropdown` branch.
- **Existing file**: the route-addressability crash that this flow was written for is owned by `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/nav/NavSavedStateConfigTest.kt` — `settings and search — the routes that crashed — are addressable` — and the tab/row mapping is owned by `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/settings/SettingsMenuTest.kt` (`every tab is a row of the menu, exactly once`) and `SettingsNavigationTest.kt` (`every settings tab belongs to exactly one group`). **No existing test renders all 13 tabs** — the desktop tier currently opens only the Calendar and Sync tabs (`clickSettingsTab("Calendar")` / `clickSettingsTab("Sync")`).
- **Must stay on-device**: the narrow sheet's open-scroll-tap mechanics and the `settings_tab_dropdown` trigger, which exists only below 600 dp and has no JVM tier that opens it.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a crash on one tab makes the whole Settings screen unusable, including the preferences above it; that is the exact bug the spec SETTINGS-03 was written for, and the route-level owner does not cover a *render* crash inside a section.
- **Confidence**: medium — the split is clear, but the headless side needs a new desktop compose case over 13 tabs (the desktop shell offers a wide rail, so it is cheap), and the flow's 105 s is the area's largest cost.

### `Maestro/flows/sync/01-change-sync-interval.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the Sync tab offers the auto-sync switch, the interval row and the "sync now" row; **tapping the interval row changes its value**; the new value takes effect without a restart and survives one.
- **Production boundary**: `SyncSettingsScreen`'s `onClick = { onIntent(SyncIntent.SetInterval(nextInterval(...))) }` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/sync/presentation/SyncSettingsScreen.kt`) → `SyncViewModel` → `SyncPrefs.setScheduledInterval(SCHEDULED_INTERVAL)` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../core/sync/SyncPrefs.kt`) → `SyncRunner` re-arming the periodic sync.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/sync/SyncSettings01ScenarioTest.kt` — `@DisplayName("SYNC-SETTINGS-01 change how often sync runs")`, `change_interval_cycles_value` — already owns the scenario id and asserts the value **changed** (`textsOfTag(...).lastOrNull()` before vs. after the tap) and that the auto-sync switch is reachable.
- **Must stay on-device**: the narrow sheet's path to the Sync tab; the "survives a restart" half of the spec is not asserted by either the flow or the desktop test — it is a gap, not a device residue.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: an interval row that renders but never writes would pass this flow; the desktop test fails it.
- **Confidence**: high for the drop of the *behaviour*; medium for the "survives a restart" clause, which nobody owns — flag it as an open item rather than as a device residue.

### `Maestro/flows/profile/01-create-profile.yaml` — replace-candidate (retired 2026-11-23, #3122)

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a second profile can be created from the profile switcher and is listed under its name (`profile_item_work`).
- **Production boundary**: `ProfileSwitcherIntent.Create(name, emoji, colorIdx)` → `ProfileSwitcherViewModel.create` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/profile/ProfileSwitcherViewModel.kt:79,131`) → `ProfileRepository` → `ProfileSwitcherScreen`'s dialog (`profile_create_name_input`, `dialog_confirm`).
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/profile/ProfileFlowTest.kt` — `create_profile_from_drawer` — `@DisplayName("PROFILE-01 …")` drives the same journey (drawer → `PROFILE_CREATE_BUTTON` → "Work" → `Dialog.CONFIRM` → `TestTags.profileItem("Work")`).
- **Must stay on-device**: the real IME into the name field and the Android menu sheet's `menu_profiles` row; `profile/02-isolation.yaml` already keeps the IME + `hideKeyboard` residue in the area. `MenuSheetBoundsTest` owns menu-sheet reachability.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a profile that is created but not listed is invisible to the user; the desktop test asserts the card, not just the intent.
- **Confidence**: high — the desktop twin asserts the identical outcome.
- **Done (2026-11-23, #3122)**: flow removed from Maestro/catalogue/qualification baselines, `PROFILE-01` spec retargeted to desktop, desktop `ProfileFlowTest` is the carrier.

### `Maestro/flows/profile/02-isolation.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a task written in profile "Work" is **not** visible under profile "Personal" **and is visible again** after switching back. The second half is the load-bearing one, and the flow does not assert it.
- **Production boundary**: `ProfileSwitcherIntent.SwitchTo(id)` → `ProfileSwitcherViewModel.switchTo` (`.../feature/profile/ProfileSwitcherViewModel.kt:120,137`) → `ProfileRepositoryImpl.switchTo` → `activeProfileState.write(id)` and `ProfileAwareCurrentUser.switchToProfileNow` (`.../feature/profile/ProfileRepositoryImpl.kt:155-166`, `.../feature/profile/ProfileAwareCurrentUser.kt:101`) → `scopedUserIdFor(profileId, userId)` (`.../feature/profile/ProfileAwareCurrentUser.kt:21`) → the scoped repository queries.
- **Existing file**: `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/tasks/TaskRepositoryContractTest.kt` — `observeAll returns only the current user's tasks` and `observeSeries is scoped to the current user` (real SQLite, a second user id sees nothing) — is the right home for the both-sided case; `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/profile/ProfileIdentityAccessorsTest.kt` — `liveScopedUserId_reflects_the_switch_immediately` — owns the switch→identity propagation; `shared/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/arch/ScopedReadQueryIsolationTest.kt` owns the static "every read is scoped" rule.
- **Must stay on-device**: the switch performed **through the app's own Profiles screen** (the flow switches on purpose, because the seed link's `profile=` parameter would switch behind the app's back), the debug seed writing into the *active* profile, and the real IME + `hideKeyboard`.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: profile isolation is the core data boundary; a build that clears the list on every switch would pass the flow's one-sided assertion and fail the both-sided one — and would silently destroy the user's data.
- **Confidence**: high for the both-sided headless case (the repository-level owners already exist and are close); medium for the "switch through the UI" residue, which is a UI concern the desktop twin would need to mirror.

### `Maestro/flows/pomodoro/02-start-focus-task.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: selecting a task chip **starts** the timer on that task — one step, no separate "start" (the spec's load-bearing word is "already"); the focus chip names the bound task.
- **Production boundary**: `PomodoroScreen.onTaskSelected = { timer.start(it) }` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/pomodoro/PomodoroScreen.kt:87`) → `PomodoroTimer.start(taskId)` → `PomodoroState(isRunning = true, taskId = taskId)` (interface at `.../feature/pomodoro/PomodoroTimer.kt:13`; `AndroidPomodoroTimer`/`JvmPomodoroTimer` implement it).
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/pomodoro/PomodoroFlowTest.kt` is the right home for the chip→start case; it currently has no chip because the desktop harness binds `EmptyPomodoroTaskListProvider` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../test/helpers/TestPlatformModule.kt:376`) — the harness change is a prerequisite, and `PomodoroFlowTest`'s own header names it.
- **Must stay on-device**: the **Android** inbox as the chip source (`PomodoroTaskListProvider` → real tasks) and the chip row's own rendering.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a chip that selects the task but does not start the timer (or starts it on the wrong task) is a silent no-op; the wiring is two lines of shared code and needs no device.
- **Confidence**: medium — the production path is shared and clear, but the desktop harness must first bind a seeded provider, and the chip's own rendering is Android-shaped.

### `Maestro/flows/pomodoro/04-skip-to-break.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: skipping a **work** block starts a **break** and advances the cycle counter ("Cycle 2"), and the completed-work counter reads "Pomodoro: 1 completed". The flow asserts only that the cycle label is *visible* before and after.
- **Production boundary**: `PomodoroTimer.skip()` → `onPhaseComplete()` → `nextPhase(state, config)` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/pomodoro/PomodoroDomain.kt:50-83`) → the rendered `text = "Cycle ${completedCycles + 1}"` and `text = "Pomodoro: ${state.completedCycles} completed"` (`.../feature/pomodoro/PomodoroScreen.kt:245,130`).
- **Existing file**: the transition rule is already owned by `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/pomodoro/PomodoroStateMachineTest.kt` — `nextPhase to ShortBreak has correct duration and increments cycles` (and `PomodoroDomainTest.nextPhase after Work cycles through ShortBreak until LongBreak`). The **UI advance** has no owner: `PomodoroFlowTest` has no skip case, and the desktop fake `TestPomodoroTimer.skip()` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../test/helpers/TestPorts.kt:282`) only clears `isRunning` — it does **not** advance the phase, so the fake must be extended before a desktop case can assert "Cycle 2".
- **Must stay on-device**: the chip-driven start and the Android timer's `AlarmManager` safety net (`AndroidPomodoroTimer` schedules a phase-end alarm; `JvmPomodoroTimer` has no alarm) — the alarm re-arming is Android-only and is asserted by no tier.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a build that increments the cycle but not the completed-work count (or the reverse) is exactly the split the spec POMODORO-04 exists to catch, and the current flow cannot catch it — it only checks that a label is on screen.
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the state-machine half (already owned); medium for the UI half (needs the fake extended, then a desktop compose case).

### `Maestro/flows/pomodoro/05-stop-resets.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: after stop the timer is idle again (the play control is offered) **and** the focus task is released (`PomodoroState.taskId` back to null; the "Focus: …" chip gone).
- **Production boundary**: `PomodoroTimer.stop()` → `initialState()` (`shared/src/jvmMain/.../feature/pomodoro/JvmPomodoroTimer.kt`, `shared/src/androidMain/.../feature/pomodoro/AndroidPomodoroTimer.kt`) → `PomodoroState(taskId = null, isRunning = false)` → the focus chip's rendering in `PomodoroScreen`.
- **Existing file**: **none owns this today.** The closest owners are `shared/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/pomodoro/PomodoroStateMachineTest.kt` (the `nextPhase` rules, not `stop()`) and `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/pomodoro/PomodoroFlowTest.kt` (play/pause only). The natural new owner is a timer-level test on `JvmPomodoroTimer` (its `Clock` and `scope` are injectable) plus a desktop compose case in `PomodoroFlowTest`.
- **Must stay on-device**: the focus chip itself — desktop has no chips, so "the chip disappears" is Android-only today.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a stop that resets the timer but keeps the task binding would satisfy the flow's first assertion and fail the second, and the user would go back to work on a task the timer no longer tracks; that is the whole point of the scenario.
- **Confidence**: medium-high for the timer-level half; the chip half stays device-only.

### `Maestro/flows/pomodoro/01-open-tab.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the timer is legible on first open — phase label ("Work"), cycle label ("Cycle 1"), timer label, stop/skip/play controls, and the control is the *play* one (paused).
- **Production boundary**: `PomodoroState`'s initial value (`phase = Work`, `completedCycles = 0`, `isRunning = false` — `shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/pomodoro/PomodoroDomain.kt:20`) → `PomodoroScreen`'s `when (isRunning)` control branch.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/pomodoro/PomodoroFlowTest.kt` — `timer_starts_paused_on_the_first_work_cycle` — asserts the phase label, "Work", "Cycle 1", the timer/stop/skip labels, `PLAY_BUTTON` present and `PAUSE_BUTTON` absent.
- **Must stay on-device**: the Android menu sheet's `menu_pomodoro` row (shared with the other pomodoro flows).
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: every later control is meaningless if the initial state cannot be read; the desktop twin already owns that.
- **Confidence**: high.

### `Maestro/flows/pomodoro/03-pause-resume.yaml` — replace-candidate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: play and pause are **one control** whose identity depends on the running state; pausing and resuming preserve the phase ("Work") and the cycle.
- **Production boundary**: `PomodoroTimer.pause()` / `resume()` → `PomodoroState(isRunning, phaseStartedAtEpochMs)` → `recomputeRemaining(state, nowEpochMs)` (`.../feature/pomodoro/PomodoroDomain.kt:92`) → the control's `when` branch in `PomodoroScreen`.
- **Existing file**: `desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/pomodoro/PomodoroFlowTest.kt` — `play_and_pause_swap_the_single_control` — asserts `PLAY_BUTTON`→`PAUSE_BUTTON`→`PLAY_BUTTON` and that the phase still reads "Work".
- **Must stay on-device**: the chip-driven start (POMODORO-02's subject, which stays) and the Android `AlarmManager` re-arm on resume.
- **Why a headless test catches what matters**: a build showing a play icon while running would satisfy "the control is visible" and still be wrong; the desktop test asserts the **swap**, the flow asserts only that the timer label is still visible.
- **Confidence**: high.

### Calendar, calendar sync, lifecycle, capture (manual)

### `calendar_sync/01-provider-toggle.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the provider segment swaps the panel; the system controls disappear when Google is selected and return when the system is selected again.
- **Production boundary**: `CalendarSyncViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/calendarSync/presentation/`) and the provider gate (the "conjunction of the four seams" that `CalendarSyncViewModelCapabilityTest` asserts).
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `CalendarSyncProviderScenarioTest.switching_provider_swaps_the_panel` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/kotlin/com/singularity/todo/feature/flows/calendar/CalendarSyncProviderScenarioTest.kt`) already asserts the same swap, and the traceability index already links `CAL-SYNC-PROVIDER-01` to that file.
- **Must stay on-device**: the flow addresses the panel by **resource id** (`calendar_sync_system_enable_switch`, `calendar_sync_provider_google_calendar`, `calendar_sync_google_connect_button`) — a tag surface no headless tier renders; and the Android **grant** path — the Google connect button only appears on a device where a Google account can be authorised. That half is what currently fails.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression**: the swap is a ViewModel state transition with a UI projection; a regression that leaves the system panel on screen while Google is selected is exactly what the desktop twin already guards, in seconds instead of a device run.
- **Why this is a `split` and not a `replace-candidate`**: the residue (the id-addressed switch and the grant path) has **no owner today**. The desktop tier asserts the **absence** of the system switch (`CalendarSyncSystemScenarioTest` → `assertTagNotExists(TestTags.CalendarSync.SYSTEM_ENABLE_SWITCH)`), because desktop has no system calendar to switch. So the flow cannot be dropped whole; only its swap half is headless-owned. A follow-up should re-scope the flow to the id-addressed surface and the grant path rather than drop it.

### `calendar_sync/02-system-calendar-unavailable.yaml` — investigate

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: when the platform cannot serve a system calendar, the panel says so plainly and draws no controls over providers that were never queried.
- **Production boundary**: `CalendarSyncViewModel`'s capability gate; the desktop ports' `isSupported = false`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `CalendarSyncSystemScenarioTest.the_system_panel_says_why_it_cannot_work` (desktop, exact wording + no enable switch + no sync-now button) and `CalendarSyncPlatformHonestyTest` (JVM).
- **Must stay on-device**: nothing — there is no Android state that produces this message.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression**: the guarantee is a desktop guarantee. On Android the message is unreachable, so the flow can only ever fail; the flow's failure is not a product regression but an unreachable assertion.
- **Confidence**: high that the guarantee is owned elsewhere and that this flow cannot pass on the target platform. It is registered as debt #835 with `owner: unclassified` — the classification is what this audit proposes to fix.

### `calendar/01-month-view.yaml`, `calendar/02-view-mode.yaml`, `calendar/03-tap-day.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: the month grid has a cell for every day of the month; the mode control opens a four-way switcher and the selection closes it; tapping a day in month view switches to the day view on that date.
- **Production boundary**: `CalendarViewModel` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../feature/calendar/`), `CalendarDateMath`, the month grid composable.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `CalendarViewModelTest` (JVM — `DayClicked in MONTH mode switches to DAY on clicked date`, `ViewModeChanged changes mode`, `GoToday resets anchor to today`) and `CalendarFlowTest` (desktop — month title, weekday columns, addressable day cells, month → week).
- **Must stay on-device**: `nav_tab_calendar` (an Android bottom-nav tab), the **popup window** whose items only exist while expanded (no headless tier renders a popup), and the pager's duplicate day numbers.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression**: every assertion here is a state or a composition fact. What the flows add over the twins is the *tag surface* — that `calendar_weekday_mon` and `calendar_view_mode_four_days` actually reach UIAutomator — which is a real but narrow guarantee.
- **Confidence**: medium-high. The desktop twin asserts the same facts as **text**, so the tag surface is currently owned by nobody; if the flow is split, the tag half must stay on-device and the tag's *existence* should get its own cheap assertion.

### `lifecycle/01-kill-restart-persist.yaml` — split

- **Behaviour-only assertions**: a row that is on screen after a restart was persisted, not held in memory.
- **Production boundary**: the Room-backed task repository (`TaskRepositoryImpl` → `TaskDao`), `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/database/`.
- **Replacement fixture/tier**: `DesktopRestartSmokeTest` (real Room, real reopen, `@Tag("slow:data")`) and `TaskRepositoryContractTest`.
- **Must stay on-device**: the **process** being killed and the app relaunching; `stopApp`/`launchApp` have no headless equivalent.
- **Why a headless test catches the regression**: a repository that kept rows in memory would pass a UI test that never restarts and fail this one. `DesktopRestartSmokeTest` already makes exactly that claim, for notes.
- **Confidence**: medium-high. The gap the split would close is a *task* (not a note) surviving a restart — the desktop fixture proves it for notes only.

## Appendix B — what could not be verified

### Agenda and empty states

- **Root cause of the failing flows is unverified.** `agenda/01-smart-lists.yaml` and `negative/05-empty-search.yaml` (both fail in every sampled run), `agenda/02-saved-views-open.yaml`, `agenda/04-saved-view-results.yaml`, `negative/01-empty-inbox.yaml` and `negative/04-empty-archive.yaml` (2026-11-21), `agenda/09-upcoming-timeline.yaml` (2026-11-21 and 2026-11-17) have failing samples; no emulator was run, so the failing step is unknown. For `01-smart-lists` the most likely suspect visible in the tree is the disagreement between the flow's own text and `AGENDA-01.yaml` (Inbox = undated only per `AgendaTabDisjointnessTest`, vs the spec's "stays in Inbox and only changes section"); for `05-empty-search` the suspect is the idle-prompt/no-results tag rendering, whose owner is missing (see below).
- **`AGENDA-01` … `AGENDA-08` and `AGENDA-10` are not actually claimed by their flows.** `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/agenda/AGENDA-0{1..8}.yaml` and `AGENDA-10.yaml` all state that the flow carries `scenario:AGENDA-XX`, but only `09-upcoming-timeline.yaml` carries a `scenario:` tag (`scenario:AGENDA-09`); the ten other agenda flows carry only `regression` and `agenda`. The same mismatch does **not** occur in the negative area, where all six flows carry their `scenario:NEGATIVE-0X` tag. I could not verify whether the traceability tooling derives the claim from the tag or from the spec's prose.
- **No headless owner asserts the search empty-state *tags*.** `SearchContentTest` (`shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/search/SearchContentTest.kt`) owns the state *decision* and says in its own header that the composable that draws it has no Compose test harness in that module; whether some other test asserts `search_empty_placeholder` / `search_empty_results` at the screen level I did not find.
- **No headless owner asserts the Tags empty state** ("No tags yet" in `TagsScreen.kt:109`); the desktop tags tests I read do not cover it. A full sweep of every desktop tags test was not performed beyond `TagsRenameUiTest.kt`, `TagsUndoFabClearanceTest.kt`, `TagsPickerSheetTest.kt` and `TagInventory.kt`.
- **No Room round-trip test for `AgendaViewEntity` was found.** `SavedAgendaViewsRepositoryImplTest` uses a fake DAO; the only real-SQLite round trip I saw in the tree is `ProjectIconRoomRoundTripTest`. Whether the saved-view row survives a real Room write/read is therefore only asserted through the device flow.
- **The Android-shell "create FAB overlapped by the shell button" case** is described in the header of `SavedViewsFlowTest.the_saved_views_list_carries_no_shell_button` as an Android-only defect ("On Android the covered button was not reachable at all"), but that desktop test renders the *desktop* shell; I found no device test that asserts the Android shell's bar does not overlap the saved-views create FAB. If the flow is trimmed, this guarantee needs an explicit owner.
- **Whether the `07-large-overdue` count "1001" is asserted anywhere else** — `AgendaOverdueSectionUiTest` asserts a synthetic "Overdue · 1500"; the real seeded count is only in the Maestro flow.
- The measured durations in the fact sheet come from passing samples only, so the cost figures for the two flows that never passed (`01-smart-lists`, `05-empty-search`) are unknown.

### Task sub-features: checklist, logbook, subtasks

- **Root cause of the failing flows is unverified.** `TASK-CHECK-01` failed on 2026-11-21 **and** on the 2026-11-23 smoke lane (it also passed on 2026-11-19 and 2026-11-17, hence n=3 and its 58 s median rests on three samples); `TASK-LOG-02` failed on 2026-11-21. No emulator was run, so the failing step is unknown. For `TASK-CHECK-01` the suspect visible in the tree is the **fixture-history** problem the flow's own header warns about ("the composer creates a task per run"), combined with the fact that the flow waits for `task_item_${RUN_ID}_grocery_run` on the **Today** tab while a task saved without a due date is undated — and `AgendaTabDisjointnessTest` states that an undated task belongs to the **Inbox** tab and to no other. If the FAB-created task has no due date, the flow's own wait is asking for a row in the wrong bucket. I did not verify whether the FAB pre-fills a due date; `docs/testing/coverage-matrix.md` row `TASK-18` says a FAB from *Upcoming* does **not** pre-fill one. This needs checking before the flow is trimmed.
- **The known debt entries are not the failing steps.** `config/docs/maestro-debt.yaml` records issue 2722 (status *accepted*) for "id: subtask_item_r<n>_child_task_one is visible", measured 2026-11-09 against `tasks/subtasks/TASK-SUB-01` on RMX3938/API 35, and issue 2819 (status *fixed*) — the spotlight-tour scrim that swallowed the helper's tap on the Inbox tab and named `tasks/subtasks/TASK-SUB-01` among the affected flows. Neither entry names `TASK-CHECK-01` or `TASK-LOG-02`, so the current failures of those two flows are not explained by the debt registry.
- **The scenario specs are stale in one place.** `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/tasks/checklist/TASK-CHECK-01.yaml` expects "the list survives leaving the task and coming back to it", but the Maestro flow never leaves the task and returns — it only ticks the checkbox. The persistence half of the spec is therefore asserted by nothing in the flow; `TaskItemListScenarioTest` (desktop) does not cover it either, although `ChecklistOrderingTest`/`ChecklistRestoreTest` cover the ordering and undo layers. The spec text and the flow should be reconciled before either is changed.
- **`TASK-SUB-01`'s spec explicitly leaves the desktop carrier unclaimed.** `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/tasks/subtasks/TASK-SUB-01.yaml` ends with "Desktop reachability is unknown … A desktop carrier can be written once the desktop equivalent of the inline add-row is verified." The inline add-row **has** since been verified on desktop (`TaskDetailEmptyChildSectionsTest` adds a subtask through `SUBTASK_ADD_INPUT`/`SUBTASK_ADD_BUTTON` and awaits "Subtasks (0/1)"), so the spec's stated blocker no longer holds — but the spec has not been updated, and `docs/testing/coverage-matrix.md` still marks `TASK-SUB-01` as Maestro(●)/desktop(—).
- **No headless owner asserts the logbook's own UI.** `LOGBOOK_ADD_NOTE_BUTTON`, `LOGBOOK_EMPTY`, `LOGBOOK_EMPTY_HINT` and `logbookNoteByTitle(title)` appear in no test file in the tree — only in `shared/src/commonMain/.../LogbookSection.kt`, `core/ui/TestTags.kt` and the tag-mapping helpers (`core/ui/TagsMd.kt`, `arch/MaestroFlowTagsTest.kt`). Everything the two logbook flows assert about the *logbook screen* is therefore unowned outside the device lane; everything they assert about the *data* is owned three times over.
- **No headless owner asserts the subtask delete control.** `TestTags.subtaskDelete(title)` (`core/ui/TestTags.kt:748`) is used in production (`feature/checklist/components/ChecklistItemRow.kt:69`) and in the tag-mapping helpers only. The coordinator-level delete/undo is owned by `TaskChildrenSlotTest`, but the UI affordance that triggers it is not.
- **The desktop shell registers the notes graph, so the logbook "+" is reachable headlessly — I did not run it.** `shared/src/jvmMain/.../feature/nav/JvmNavEntries.kt:92` calls `notesEntries(nav, stacks)`, and `feature/nav/entries/NotesEntries.kt` registers `AppDestination.NotesGraph` for both platforms. Whether the *desktop* `TasksNavigator` path from a task detail to the notes editor behaves like the Android one (which exits the tasks nested graph) was not verified — the desktop tests I read stop at `awaitTag(TestTags.LOGBOOK_SECTION)`.
- **The IME guarantees the flows assert weakly are already owned.** `androidApp/src/androidTest/.../ui/TaskDetailImeInsetsTest.kt` focuses `CHECKLIST_INLINE_ADD_INPUT` (scroll anchor `TASK_CHECKLIST_SECTION`) and `SUBTASK_ADD_INPUT` (anchor `SUBTASKS_SECTION`) and asserts each stays above the IME and below the status bar. The flows' `assertVisible` after `inputText` is therefore a weaker duplicate of a device test that already exists — but note that the flows' `assertVisible` also covers "the field accepts the typed text while the IME is up", which the insets test does not assert.
- **No android-host test touches these features.** Greps over `shared/src/androidHostTest` for checklist / logbook / subtask found no matches, so there is no platform-without-device owner for any of the four flows.
- The measured durations come from passing samples only, so the cost of a run of `TASK-CHECK-01` (n=3) and of `TASK-LOG-02` (n=3) rests on three measurements each, and the cost of a failing run of either is unknown.

### Tasks

1. **Measured durations and statuses come from the fact sheet**, not from a re-run; I did not
   run Maestro, Gradle or an emulator (audit constraint). The 6 flows with no passing sample
   are red *in the measured window* — I could not determine **why** they fail (a broken
   selector, a device-only flake, a genuine production bug, or a stale APK). Their disposition
   as `investigate` is deliberate: they guarantee nothing today, but the *right* fix is
   diagnosis, not deletion — except where a headless owner already exists (03, 09, 14, 15, 19,
   toggle-task-complete), where deletion loses no coverage.
2. **`create-task.yaml`'s ownership**: the ownership table names Maestro as the owner of the
   FAB → title → save → row scenario, with the device instrumented copy as a *named exception*.
   The tree supports the behaviour split (desktop `CreateTaskFlowTest.kt` owns the behaviour,
   `A/CreateTaskFlowTest.kt` duplicates it deliberately). I did **not** dispute the row — but the
   flow that the row names as the owner has failed in all 5 sampled runs, so the table currently
   describes an ownership no green flow delivers. That is an operational finding for the owner,
   not a disposition change.
3. **`13-create-full-task.yaml`'s closing comment** refers to a desktop class
   `CreateFullTaskFlowTest` for the priority-badge verification. **No such class exists** in
   `desktopApp/src/jvmTest` (the closest is `CreateTaskFlowTest.kt`). I did not verify whether
   the priority badge is asserted anywhere on the desktop; I could not find a badge assertion
   for the created row. Flagged, not claimed.
4. **The 8 attribute rows** of flow 13 (`task_editor_{priority,due,estimate,start_date,project,tags,recurrence,pin}_row`) are exactly `CreateAndEditFieldParityTest.SHARED_ROWS`; that test also lists `TASK_EDITOR_REMINDER_ROW`, `TASK_EDITOR_DEADLINE_ROW`, `TASK_EDITOR_WORKFLOW_STATE_ROW` and `TASK_EDITOR_MORE_PARAMETERS_ROW` as **one-sided** (detail-only). So on the *create* surface the reminder and deadline rows do not exist by design — consistent with flows 20 and 21, which reach them from an existing task's detail, not from the composer.
5. **`task_scheduling_sheet`'s and `task_reminder_sheet`'s constants are not referenced by any
   Kotlin test** (`TASK_SCHEDULING_SHEET`, `TASK_REMINDER_SHEET` → no hits in the four test
   trees); the sheet-level tests reach the composables directly (`TaskSchedulingSheetTest`,
   `TaskReminderSheetTest` mount the sheet, not the row). I could not verify from the tree
   whether the sheet's *own* tag is asserted anywhere; the flows are currently its only
   consumers.
6. **`task_context_menu_sheet` and the three `task_action_*` ids** have no Kotlin counterpart
   in any tier: `TASK_CONTEXT_MENU_SHEET` appears only in `SJ/core/ui/TagsMd.kt` (the tag
   catalogue) and `TASK_CONTEXT_MENU_{ARCHIVE,OPEN,COMPLETE}` appear in no test at all. Flow
   05 is therefore the only assertion of the Android long-press sheet's content — which is why
   it is a `keep`.
7. **`AgendaEvaluatorTest.kt` / `AgendaEvaluatorMatrixTest.kt`** exist in
   `shared/src/commonTest/.../feature/agenda/domain/`; I cite them only as the agenda-bucket
   rule's home and did not read their individual test names.
8. I did not verify the **`Maestro/TAGS.md` catalogue** against the fact sheet's suite
   membership; the suite/tags column is taken from the fact sheet and from the `tags:` blocks
   inside the flows. The **shard plan** (`config/docs/e2e-shards.txt`) *was* checked: the Shard
   column was computed with the runner's own `select_suite_flows` and cross-checked against
   `scripts/e2e-shard-plan.py --json` for all eight shards, and the two agree.

### Auth, backup, attachments, archive

- **The root cause of the failing flows is unverified.** No emulator was run. Failures observed in the CI artefacts: `attachments/01` → `Assertion is false: "Example Article" is visible`; `attachments/05` → `Assertion is false: "https://example.com/render-test" is visible`; `attachments/02` → `Assertion is false: "https://example.com/to-delete" is visible`; `attachments/04` → `Assertion is false: "https://example.com/persist" is visible`; `auth/05` → `Assertion is false: id: auth_sign_out_button is visible`; `backup/02` → `Assertion is false: id: backup_push is visible`; `backup/01` (smoke-2026-11-23) → `Assertion is false: id: nav_tab_today is visible`.
- **The attachments failures and `auth/05` are engine-sensitive, not established product defects.** `docs/decisions/2026-11-17-maestro-runner-is-a-second-engine.md` records that `attachments/01`, `04` and `05` fail under the Maestro CLI engine and pass under the runner engine on the same builds, and `config/docs/maestro-flow-qualification.yaml` records all five attachments flows plus `auth/05` as qualified on 2026-11-23 on a physical realme RMX3938 (API 35, commit `344998aeaf23`, Maestro 2.10.0) with **3/3 passing attempts**. I did not re-run any of them; the classification "investigate" for `auth/05` and "the CI failures are environmental" for the attachments flows rests on that file and the ADR, not on my own run.
- **`backup/02`'s failure is a flow bug, not a product defect.** `backup_push` is the push button of a *local backup row* (`BackupScreen.kt:498-503`); since backup creation moved to SAF, no local row is created, so the id cannot appear. The flow's own header records this ("создание бэкапа через SAF больше не оставляет локальную строку с `backup_push`; сценарий требует переработки"). What is still unproven is whether the *password dialog* itself is reachable in any build — the flow never got that far.
- **`auth/05`'s failure is not registered in the debt registry.** `config/docs/maestro-debt.yaml` has an accepted cause for `Assertion is false: id: auth_save_server_button is visible` (issue 867, 2 flows) and for `id: dialog_confirm` (issue 2721), but nothing for `auth_sign_out_button`. Either the failure is new or it is a scroll/timeout artefact; the flow scrolls to a button at the bottom of a long Account column and a wait that names a position says nothing about whether the button exists.
- **`auth/04`'s central assertion is vacuous in the E2E build.** The stub answers a sign-in with a success but establishes **no session** (`shared/src/androidMain/.../test/stubs/TestAuthStubs.kt`), so after the form sign-in the app is still in the anonymous session and the seeded task survives trivially. The flow's own header concedes that "the form submission cannot be seen to succeed". The scenario it names (`AUTH-SEED-01`) therefore has no real carrier at any tier today.
- **`auth/10`'s comments contradict the implementation twice.** The flow says the submit button is *disabled* while the fields are empty, but `AttachAccountScreen.kt:297` enables it as soon as a disposition is picked (`submitEnabled = disposition != null`); it also says "Supabase requires min 6 chars" while `AuthDomain.MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` is **8**. The flow's assertions still hold (the submit is refused at submit time, by `LocalAccountClaimer`), but the spec text and the code disagree.
- **Traceability holes in this area.** `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/archive/ARCHIVE-01.yaml` and `.../backup/BACKUP-01.yaml` name `archive/01-restore.yaml` and `backup/01-round-trip.yaml` as their carriers, but neither flow carries a `scenario:` tag — `traceability validate` (run from `scripts/src`) lists `ARCHIVE-01 / android` and `BACKUP-01 / android` as holes. Every attachment and auth flow in the area does carry its tag.
- **No Room round trip for attachment rows was found.** `AttachmentStorageTest` (`shared/src/commonTest/.../core/attachments/`) exercises a fake filesystem; I did not find a real-SQLite test that writes an attachment row and reads it back. The persistence claim in `attachments/04` is therefore held only by the device flow (and, today, that flow is red in CI).
- **The accepted debt entry for `attachments_add_file_row` does not explain today's attachments failures.** `config/docs/maestro-debt.yaml` registers `No visible element found: id: attachments_add_file_row` (issue 818, 5 flows) as an accepted cause, but all four failing attachments flows fail *earlier*, on the attachment's own title/URL text, not on the add-file row.
- **`attachments/05`'s last assertion contradicts its own comment.** The comment says "the attachment card in the section has link icon (not image/file icon)" while the assertion checks that the *add-file row* is visible — two different claims, and the file-picker row is platform material rather than a link-icon guarantee.
- **`archive/01`'s restore half may be blocked by an open defect.** The device overflow test's header states that restoring the task is issue #2723's and is deliberately not asserted there. If #2723 is a live product defect, a new headless owner would fail on it — which is the correct outcome for a test, but it means the carve-up needs a decision about whether the flow stays red on purpose.
- **The measured costs are medians over passing samples only.** Five flows have no passing sample at all (`attachments/01`, `02`, `04`; `auth/05`; `backup/02`), `attachments/05` has exactly one and `backup/01` has three. The flows without a passing sample carry **no** cost figure at all, and for the `split` rows the saving is **unknown** on top of that — the per-flow times cannot say how much of a flow's seconds belong to its behaviour half.

### Notes, search, projects

- **The failing flows' root causes are unverified.** `projects/04-pick-emoji.yaml`, `projects/05-custom-emoji.yaml`, `search/02-cyrillic.yaml`, `search/07-saved-search.yaml`, `search/12-no-results-recovery.yaml` and `search/13-suggestion-dismissal.yaml` failed in **both** full runs and have no passing sample; `notes/01-edit-body.yaml` failed the three most recent runs (it passes in the two older ones) and the smoke run. No emulator was run, so the failing step of each is unknown. Seven red flows in one area is more consistent with a common cause (a build, a seed helper, or the app-menu path) than with seven independent defects, but I could not verify that from the tree.
- **Cost figures for those flows are unknown.** The fact sheet's durations come from passing samples only, so a never-passing flow has **no** cost figure at all: its recorded duration is the time *to the failure*, which is not a runtime measurement — a flow that dies on a timeout can burn more wall clock than a green run. Its real cost stays unknown until a passing execution is measured.
- **No flow in this area carries a notes or projects scenario id.** `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/notes/NOTES-0{1,2,3}.yaml` and `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/projects/PROJECTS-0{1,2}.yaml` each state that they are "carried by Maestro flow … (`scenario:NOTES-XX` / `scenario:PROJECTS-XX`)", but none of the four notes flows and none of the five projects flows carries a `scenario:` tag — only `search`, `smoke`, `regression`, `notes`, `projects` and `i18n`. This is the same mismatch area A found for the agenda flows, and it is why every flow in the fact sheet reports "kotlin carriers for the same scenario id: (none)". I could not determine whether the traceability tooling derives the claim from the tag or from the spec's prose.
- **Scenario ids that no flow in this area claims**: `SEARCH-01` is carried by `Maestro/flows/system/search-finds-task.yaml` (outside this area); `SEARCH-09` (target: desktop) and `SEARCH-11` (target: desktop) have no carrier flow here — their owners appear to be the desktop highlight and pending-state tests. `search/09-contains-mode.yaml` carries `scenario:SEARCH-10`, so the file name and the scenario id disagree.
- **Two flows claim the same search empty-state behaviour**: `search/06-no-results.yaml` and `negative/05-empty-search.yaml` (area A) both assert the idle prompt vs the no-results state. Only one of them should keep the device slot; the headless owner `SearchContentTest` is shared.
- **The empty-state *tags* have no headless owner.** `search_empty_placeholder` and `search_empty_results` are asserted by device flows only; `SearchContentTest`'s own header says the composable that draws them has no Compose harness in that module. The same gap is flagged in area A.
- **The notes' Pinned/Archived narrowing has no headless owner.** `NotesListViewModelTest` asserts the chip *state* (`setFilter updates filter state`) but not that the observed set changes; `NotesFlowTest` asserts only that the chips are offered. The mapping in `NotesListViewModel.kt:184-186` is untested.
- **The tap on an internal link in a note body has no headless owner.** `NotesNavigatorTest` owns the navigation; nothing asserts that tapping the anchor reaches it, and no test asserts that the note preview renders a `task://` anchor as a link.
- **No test asserts that a note body survives a process restart on Android.** The note editor's body→note path is desktop-owned, but no Room round-trip test for the note body was found, and the only device proof is `notes/01-edit-body.yaml` — which passes in the two older sampled runs and fails in the three most recent ones.
- **The provider's category label ("Faces") has no owner.** `projects/04-pick-emoji.yaml` asserts that the sheet opens on `SmileysAndPeopleCategory`'s own English name; no headless test asserts it, and the sheet does not keep its own labels.
- **Two heads of the search suggestion lifecycle are not clearly owned**: the Save chip's *continued visibility* after an outside-tap dismissal (`search/13-suggestion-dismissal.yaml` asserts it; `SearchSuggestionInteractionUiTest` covers the dialog opening after a dismissal instead), and the saved-search read-back after leaving and re-entering the screen (`search/07-saved-search.yaml`).
- **The `05-custom-emoji` disposition follows the named exception verbatim.** `docs/architecture/test-tier-ownership.md` gives the *real IME* to this flow (rows 37 and 121), the rule to `CustomEmojiInputTest`, the desktop field to `IconPickerCustomEmojiTest` and the Android sheet to `IconPickerCustomInputTest`. If that flow is trimmed, the IME guarantee is lost — the row would have to be amended first.
- **Cost model.** The area's 15 green flows cost 35–87 s each (median 50 s), so the area's green runs are roughly 13 minutes of device time per full suite; the six red flows add an unmeasured amount on top of that.

### Menu, navigation, system

- **The root cause of `menu/09-attach-account-entry.yaml`'s two failures is not pinned down.** Both full runs fail with the identical behaviour assertion `Assertion is false: id: menu_attach_account is visible`. Two candidate causes remain open, and no run was performed to separate them: (a) the `session=anonymous` seed link (`helpers/seed.yaml` → `todo-debug://seed?session=anonymous` → `DebugSeedActivity.switchSession`, which calls `authRepository.startLocalSession()` → `StubAuthRepository` sets `Session.Anonymous`) does not land **before** the sheet is composed, so `rememberIsAnonymousSession()` was still `SignedIn` at composition; or (b) the sheet was composed while the session was still signed in and the `showAttachAccount` flag never re-evaluated on the session change. The flow is red in every measured sample and the gate has no Kotlin owner, so the fix belongs in a test that owns the gate, not in the flow.
- **Whether the `MENU-04` contradiction is a bug in the flow, in the desktop carrier, or in the traceability spec** — the spec file `scripts/src/traceability/scenarios/menu/MENU-04.yaml` was not re-read line by line in this session, only its "Calendar is listed in the menu on both platforms" expectation as reported by the fact sheet. The claim that `ProjectsMenuCalendarScenarioTest` passes because the **drawer** draws `DestinationKind.tabs` (which includes Calendar) is an inference from `DesktopShellNav3.kt` and `DestinationKind.tabs`; it was not confirmed by executing the desktop test.
- **Whether `menu_calendar` was ever applied to any surface.** The tag is declared at `core/ui/TestTags.kt:192` and listed as known-unapplied in `shared/src/jvmTest/.../arch/TestTagsWiringTest.kt`, and `AppMenu.allDestinations` excludes Calendar — but "declared but never applied" was read from those two files, not from an exhaustive search of every `testTag(` call site. If some surface still applies it, the flow's negative assertion is meaningful and the disposition should move from `investigate` to `keep`.
- **The exact failure text of `nav/deep-link-task.yaml` on 2026-11-21 and of `menu/07-ai-chat-entry.yaml` on 2026-11-21 was not read.** Only the pass/fail statuses were available from the fact sheet and the run summaries, so whether those were behaviour failures or harness failures is unknown. By contrast, the 2026-11-23 smoke failures of `nav/bottom-nav-tabs` and `system/search-finds-task` were read from the JUnit XML and are the harness symptom `Assertion is false: id: nav_tab_today is visible` (the `launch-clean` helper's 30 s wait).
- **`menu/01-projects-roots.yaml`'s failure on 2026-11-22 was not diagnosed.** Its three passing samples have an 88 s median (82–95 s); the failure text was not read, so it is unknown whether the roots-only guarantee broke or the flow hit a timeout. Given that it is the slowest flow in the area, the flake itself deserves attention before any decision to keep it as-is.
- **The `system/seed-unknown-key.yaml` flow does not assert the logcat line it documents.** The header claims the unknown key "is reported by name in the logcat (`Unknown seed query key 'blockeddBy'`)", but the flow only asserts that `task_item_buy_milk` is visible — the report itself is unasserted on-device as well.
- **Instrumented-test coverage for `MENU_ATTACH_ACCOUNT` is conditional.** `A/MenuSheetBoundsTest.kt` asserts that tag only *if rendered*, so the device tier cannot currently distinguish "the entry is correctly hidden for a signed-in session" from "the entry is missing because the gate is broken". This was read from the file's `requiredMenuTags()` helper, not from an executed test.
- **The desktop tier's coverage of the proposals destination was searched for but not found** — no class referencing `menu_proposals` or `proposals_empty` exists in the trees read here. Whether an equivalent class exists under a different name (asserting the destination rather than the tag) was not established.
- **No cost measurement was re-run.** All durations are the passing samples from the fact sheet; the proposed replacements' own runtimes were not measured, so the net saving of the `replace-candidate` dispositions is an estimate, not a measurement.

### Smoke, settings, sync, profile, pomodoro

- **No tier asserts that the narrow Settings sheet opens.** `TestTags.Settings.TAB_DROPDOWN` (`shared/src/commonMain/.../core/ui/TestTags.kt:1216`) is referenced by no test in `shared/src/jvmTest`, `shared/src/commonTest`, `desktopApp/src/jvmTest` or `androidApp/src/androidTest`; the desktop shell renders the wide nav rail and opens tabs through `clickSettingsTab` (`desktopApp/src/jvmTest/.../test/helpers/DesktopInteractions.kt:205`). So the narrow-sheet mechanics that `smoke/02`, `smoke/03-account`, `settings/03`, `sync/01-change-sync-interval` and `profile/02` all depend on have **no headless owner**, and I cannot say whether one could be written at the desktop tier without checking whether the desktop shell renders a narrow variant.
- **The overdue health bucket has no drill-down owner.** `StatisticsHealthTabFlowTest` asserts `HEALTH_BUCKET_OVERDUE` is displayed, `AgendaSectionTagReachabilityFlowTest` clicks only the blocked and stagnant buckets, and `AgendaOverdueSectionUiTest` asserts the Overdue *agenda section* but never via the health bucket. I could not verify whether some other tier clicks it.
- **Three flows assert less than their scenario spec requires**, and I did not run them to confirm the weak assertion is what actually executes: `pomodoro/04-skip-to-break.yaml` asserts the cycle label's *visibility* (never its value), `sync/01-change-sync-interval.yaml` asserts the interval row's visibility (never that the value changed), and `profile/02-isolation.yaml` ends at the one-sided `assertNotVisible` although spec PROFILE-02 demands the both-sided claim. Reading the YAML is not the same as observing the run.
- **`POMODORO-04` and the flow's own comment disagree with the code.** The flow comments that "Pomodoro: 1 completed" only appears after a real work session, not after a skip, but `nextPhase` increments `completedCycles` on a Work→break transition and `PomodoroScreen.kt:130` renders `"Pomodoro: ${state.completedCycles} completed"`. I read the code, not a running app; the spec POMODORO-04 sides with the code.
- **The scenario↔flow linkage is inconsistent in this area.** Specs POMODORO-01, POMODORO-04, PROFILE-02, SETTINGS-01/02/03 and SYNC-LOCAL-01 all name a flow that carries the corresponding `scenario:` tag, but those flows carry no such tag (only POMODORO-02, POMODORO-03, POMODORO-05 and SYNC-SETTINGS-01 do). `PROFILE-01` was retired on 2026-11-23 (#3122); its desktop JVM carrier carries the scenario id via `@DisplayName`. I did not check whether `run-maestro` or the traceability tooling reads the spec files' names instead of the tags, so I cannot say whether the linkage is broken or merely redundant.
- **The cost column is the median of the passing samples only.** Six flows in this area failed in at least one sampled run — `smoke/02` (2026-11-23) and `smoke/03-health`, `smoke/06`, `settings/03`, `profile/02`, `pomodoro/01` (2026-11-21; `smoke/06` failed in two of them — 2026-11-21 and 2026-11-17); their failing steps are unverified, and a `replace-candidate` on a flow whose real cost is a time-to-failure is not a cost claim.
- **Whether a `shared/src/jvmTest` Compose test is possible** — I found no `createComposeRule`/`runComposeUiTest` in `shared/src/jvmTest`, so "headless" in this report means either a plain JVM test in `shared/src/{jvmTest,commonTest}` or a desktop Compose test in `desktopApp/src/jvmTest`. The two are not interchangeable: `nextPhase` is `internal` to the shared module, while `TestPomodoroTimer` is desktop-only.
- **The `AndroidPomodoroTimer`'s `AlarmManager` re-arming has no owner in any tier I read** (`shared/src/androidHostTest` has `AndroidNotifierTest`, `NotificationPermissionRequesterTest`, `AndroidGraphResolutionTest` — no pomodoro scheduler test). Whether that is in scope for this area or for the device tier is a policy call I cannot make.
- **`Maestro/CONVENTIONS.md` and `Maestro/TAGS.md` were not re-read line by line** in this session; the flow tags quoted in the table come from the flow files themselves and the fact sheet.

### Calendar, calendar sync, lifecycle, capture (manual)

- **No emulator was run.** Every disposition for this area rests on reading flows and test classes, not on observing the flows. The `investigate` on `calendar_sync/02` and the `keep` on `lifecycle/03-rotate-in-editor` would be strengthened by one device run each.
- **`calendar_sync/01-provider-toggle` fails with `calendar_sync_google_connect_button is visible`.** Whether the cause is a missing grant, a renamed tag or an unrendered panel was **not** determined — that needs the device. The registered debt registry has no entry for that exact key (the closest is `calendar_sync_system_enable_switch`, issue #835), so the cause may be new.
- **`calendar/01` and `calendar/03` are intermittent, not stable failures** (`calendar/01` failed in 1 of 4 sampled full runs plus the 2026-11-23 smoke lane; `calendar/03` failed in 3 of 4 sampled full runs). The cause was not isolated.
- **The desktop twins assert text, not tags.** `CalendarFlowTest` asserts "Mon"/"Sun"/"Month"; the flows assert `calendar_weekday_mon`/`calendar_view_mode_month`. Whether the *tags* are a promise the product intends to keep is a product decision, not a test decision; this audit records the difference and does not resolve it.
- **`CalendarSyncProviderScenarioTest` reads `providerSegment("Google Calendar")`**, i.e. the tag is derived from the label the app shows. The flow derives it from the *literal* `calendar_sync_provider_google_calendar`. If the app's label changes, the flow breaks while the twin keeps working — a divergence worth noting, not a defect found here.
